The minor stays 1.12.

| Effect | 1.12 clause on | Class, reason, §11 bullet | C-ABI witness |
|---|---|---|---|
| During `fixpp_engine_stop`, on the route where an inbound frame's processing meets the cancellation, `fixpp_session_is_established` turns false before the peer's connection closes, where the connection closed first (§9) | none | **Additive under `[const §X.7]`: a change of output the documentation leaves unspecified.** The fact: at the base, on the thrown timing, `Engine::stop` closes the transport at its step 2, and the session's state, with the flag that `CapiApplication::onLogout` stores, changes at its step 4, after the join (read from `Engine::stop` and from `src/capi/engine.cpp`). After D-CAN-1 the flag turns false at the handler, and the pump's own close closes the transport next (measured for the C++ path, Probe 2). The article: a breaking change is one after which a consumer "sees a call it could already make fail or return a result other than the one documented", and "Adding a symbol, a constant, or an error code in an unused slot is additive, and so is changing output the documentation leaves unspecified." The declaration of `fixpp_session_is_established` in `include/fix/c_api/session.h` documents its reentrancy and its cost, and has no sentence that orders the flag against the peer's end-of-stream during `fixpp_engine_stop` (read). Every other observer reads the same on both orders: `fixpp_engine_stop` returns after the session is closed; `fixpp_session_is_established` reads false after it; the peer reads the same bytes and the same end-of-stream (measured for the C++ path, T0A.8 and Probe 2); the C application has no logout callback, and no receive or send callback runs between the two instants. Reach, reasoned from T0A.7's trace: a C engine stopped while a resend answer is being written can meet the thrown timing, on the store a C session runs on | **None, and the reason:** the one observer of the order is a second thread that polls the flag while another thread or process reads the socket, and no C-visible event separates the two instants. Both orders occur under `fixpp_engine_stop` before and after this batch: the ordinary stop, which cancels a parked read (D-CAN-6, case 1), closes the connection first. So no C program can rely on either order. The `version.h` entry and the B&L row record the effect as a change of unspecified output |
| A cancellation's returned code from `on_inbound_frame` (D-CAN-4) | none | **No reach.** No C function receives the result of `on_inbound_frame`: `git grep -n 'on_inbound_frame(' -- src/capi bindings` matches nothing; the engine's pump tests only whether the result holds a value | none is possible |
| An outbound frame whose store call is cancelled is not transmitted, the send fails and the session ends, where on one timing it was transmitted and the send succeeded (§10) | none | **No reach of the timing whose ending changes.** That is the returned timing, and it needs a store call queued at the store's lock. (1) A C session runs on `MemoryStoreFactory` (`src/capi/config.cpp`), whose store's lock never queues on one serialisation domain, so its `store` never returns `store_cancelled` (the last row of the table above gives the reading). (2) `fixpp_session_send` and `fixpp_session_close` spawn their coroutines with `asio::use_future` (`src/capi/session.cpp`), a token with no cancellation slot, so a C caller cannot cancel either; nothing in the engine emits on a send's thread (T0A.12, read). (3) `Engine::stop`'s emit is total, and after the number is assigned the thread's filter is terminal, so it is not recorded ahead of a `MemoryStore`'s lock. **The thrown timing** is not reached through C either. It needs a terminal cancellation recorded inside the one yield `MemoryStore::store` makes before its lock. The one emit of that type on a thread that can be in an outbound store call is the race of `Session::close` on its logout phase, when the grace timer expires or a terminal close escalates, and it is recorded after the Logout's store call has passed its lock: the reading is in the last row of this table. Were it reached, its ending would be the same before and after for every C observer of the close: no Logout on the wire, `fixpp_session_close` returns at once, `fixpp_session_is_established` is false (read from `run_logout_phase1`: today the phase's wait throws at its pre-check; after D-OUT-6 the phase leaves before it). Both codes translate to `FIXPP_ERR_CANCELLED` (`src/capi/error.cpp`) | none is possible; the C++ cells stand, and the B&L row says so |
| `fixpp_session_close` returns without waiting the logout timeout when its Logout was not sent (D-OUT-6) | none | **No reach of a wait that D-OUT-6 shortens.** Outcome by outcome. A cancelled store and a store failure that fails closed: no reach, a C session's store is a `MemoryStore`, which is not persistent and whose lock does not queue. A Logout that cannot be built: no reach (D-OUT-6's cell row). A failed write: on the TCP transports the C ABI builds, a write fails only after the connection was reset or closed, and that ends the read pump's parked read with an error as well; the pump's `close(terminal)` then escalates the graceful close and ends its wait at once, today as after (the behaviour `InboundFrameDispositionsOd29`'s cells pin). So a C caller reaches the early return and no shorter call. Reasoned from the transports and from `Session::close`'s escalation; not run. `fixpp_session_close`'s return code does not change | none is possible; the C++ cell "D-OUT-6, the other outcomes" stands |
| A `fixpp_session_send` that D-OUT-10's compare stops: its frame is not transmitted and the call fails, where the frame could be transmitted and the call succeed (D-OUT-10) | none | **No reach.** The compare stops a send only after a writer has lowered the session's next outbound number under it, and under a C session no writer of D-OUT-10's table does that while a send is in its store call. **The take-back** needs a cancelled outbound store on the `MemoryStore` a C session runs on, which needs a terminal cancellation recorded inside the one yield `MemoryStore::store` makes before its lock. A C caller cannot emit one, and `Engine::stop`'s emit is total (the row above on a cancelled store, (2) and (3)). The one emit of that type is the race of `Session::close` on its logout phase. **The reading of that race** (the round's triage, C-2; re-derived for this rewrite from the sources of the recipe; reasoned from those readings, not run): `Session::close` runs the phase as the first arm of a race with its grace timer, and an escalating close cancels that timer. The race is a parallel group of two `co_spawn`s on the session's executor, launched in index order in one call, and a spawned coroutine starts by a dispatch, which runs inline on its own executor. So the phase's body runs inline, in the handler that starts the race, as far as its first post: it builds the Logout, calls `toAdmin`, takes the sequence-number lock, which is granted inline when it is free, and enters `MemoryStore::store`, whose first statement posts. The timer's wait is started after that, in the same call. The group cancels the phase's thread from the handler of the arm that completed, and the timer's arm completes through a handler that is queued when the timer is cancelled or expires: a timer's cancel moves the pending wait to the scheduler's queue and completes nothing inline. That handler is queued on the session's executor after the store's post, and the executor runs handlers in the order they were queued. So when the cancellation is recorded on the phase's thread, the Logout's store call has resumed and passed its lock; it is not a cancelled store. The same holds for the timer's natural expiry. **The other lowering writers:** `src/capi/config.cpp` sets `reset_on_logon` and no other reset knob, so a C session's close runs no teardown reset; a reset unit and the `reset_on_logon` reset run in the Logon arms, where `Session::send` refuses because the state is not `Active`; 059's reconcile runs on a persistent store only. **Recipe:** asio at the version the build compiles: `operator||` in `experimental/awaitable_operators.hpp`; `parallel_group_launch` and the group's per-operation handler in `experimental/impl/parallel_group.hpp`; `co_spawn_entry_point` in `impl/co_spawn.hpp`; the reactor's `cancel_timer`; the strand's `enqueue`. Then `Session::close`'s race, `run_logout_phase1` up to its store call, `MemoryStore::store`, and the lock operation's uncontended path in `async_mutex.hpp`. That no other emit of a terminal cancellation exists: `grep -rn -E '\.emit\(' src include`, every hit read for its type, and the uses of `awaitable_operators` in `src`, each read for whether an arm can be in an outbound store call | none: there is no C-reachable effect to witness. The reading is tested by an implementation step with a return condition (§5): if the Logout's store call is shown cancelled there, the reach is real, `fixpp_session_send` joins the 1.12 clause, and the owner is told before the fix is kept |

- **Exits of §4.7 with no C-ABI witness, and why.** The validate gate's exit: a C session cannot turn the gate on (`git grep -c validate_inbound_messages -- src/capi` matches nothing; the control `heartbeat_interval` matches). The Reset-mode arm's `fromAdmin`-rejected exit: the C ABI's application, `CapiApplication`, does not override `fromAdmin`. Both have C++ cells (V, S1), and the B&L row lists them as without a C-ABI witness. The `session_already_closed` exit: it needs a frame delivered while the session's sequence-number mutex is drained or cancelled, which happens in the session's own teardown and which a peer cannot arrange. Its helper call is the out-of-sequence site's, which the forms of witness (1) cover.
- **D-537-9 changes nothing a C caller can observe.** The frames of that pattern keep a session established today as well.
- **`[const §X.7]`'s other duties.** BREAKING is marked on each declaration, in the PR description and in the B&L delta. Every in-repository consumer is updated in the same PR; re-derive the sites that encode the minor with `git grep -n -l -i -E 'abi.?minor|VERSION_MINOR' -- include src tests bindings tools` and classify each.
- **The Appendix A controls** (`[const §X.6]`, `[const §X.7]`, `[const §XI.7]`; Appendix A rows: ABI surface, threading / concurrency, session FSM), met as `.specify/495-493-486-dict-reify-copy.md` §0.2 met them in issue mode:
  - Codex Gate A: this loop;
  - `/clarify`: by hand. §0's rulings are its record;
  - `/analyze`: run over this note by the spec-analyzer after Gate A converges and before implementation. Not yet run;
  - the owner's `/plan` sign-off: the owner's approval of the converged note, which serves as the plan. Given on 2026-10-11.

### 6.3 Hot path (`[const §VIII.5]`)

No violation was found. Read and reasoned:

- The deadline helper's call sites, the Heartbeat rules (D-537-6, D-537-8) and the Logout's text are in slow sub-arms of `on_inbound_active_`: the validate-failed arm, the Reject branches of PossDup Stage 1, the Reset-mode arm, the too-high arm and the out-of-sequence arm. None is on the path of an in-sequence frame that is accepted. The text is built in a stack buffer.
- The refresh is on the in-sequence path, inside `close_filled_resend_gap_`, as a second statement after that function's existing test. It runs only while a resend is outstanding and the session has a clock, and it adds one `steady_now()` read there. **Condition: the refresh stays behind the "a resend is outstanding" test**, so a session with no gap pays the flag read it already pays and nothing more.
- The logout wake is a `std::function` that captures one reference, as `close_grace_wake_` does, on the close path.
- The recovery window's global-heap half is disclosed, not gated (`tests/perf/test_session_recovery_alloc_guard.cpp`, `L-497-1`).

§9 and §10 (review rounds and dispositions are logged under `## Gate A`; the owner signed off this loop on 2026-10-12):

- **D-CAN-1's `try` is on the path of every inbound frame.** It adds no coroutine frame and no statement to a path that does not throw; what it can change is the size of `on_inbound_active_`'s frame, which is cycled on every in-sequence frame and must stay under the recycler's limit.
  - **Measured (Probe 1), for the candidate** (the `try`, a handler that sets a flag, the reset, the state write and the return; without D-CAN-5's kept entry state and test): the interceptor registrations of W-A, W-B and W-E read zero on `linux-gcc-release` and on `linux-clang-release`. The frame's size, read from the coroutine's ramp, did not change on clang and grew on gcc, and stayed under the limit on both. The verify record has the readings.
  - **Which gates can see this frame.** W-A, W-B and W-E: with a positive control that grows the frame past the limit, those three registrations fail on both presets, at the frame's own size. W-D-W's registrations pass under the same control: they run a replica pair and the send chain, so their result says nothing about `on_inbound_active_`.
  - **Not measured:** the final form, which adds one kept state and one test after the handler (an implementation step, §5); the cost of the `try` on the path that does not throw (no benchmark was run for this note; one runs in the verification phase, §5, "Gates"; that an unthrown `try` costs nothing at run time is reasoned from the table-driven exception model, not read); debug, sanitizer and libc++ builds, where the interceptor registrations do not exist; MSVC, which has no interceptor.
- **D-CAN-4's change to `persist_inbound_advance_`** is in its failure branch.
- **D-OUT-2 and D-OUT-3 are in `store_then_emit`'s failure branch.** A store call that succeeds runs the statements it runs today, plus the test of the flag that the block of D-OUT-2 reads, plus D-OUT-10's compare. Read: the test of a returned `store_cancelled` is behind `!store_r.has_value()`.
  - **D-OUT-10's compare is on the path of every stored frame that goes on to the transmit step.** It is one read of the counter's field through `peek_outbound()`, which is not a coroutine, and one compare of two integers; it has no await and no local that lives across a suspension.
  - **Measured (Probe 8), for the take-back and the compare with the returns of today's code,** on `linux-gcc-release` and `linux-clang-release`: the interceptor registrations of W-A, W-B, W-E and W-D-W's real-chain twin pass. `store_then_emit`'s frame is one of the frames of the send chain that W-E and the real-chain twin count; a positive control, a large local kept across the store's await, fails W-E, the real-chain twin and the veto registration, so those gates see this frame. The frame's size is the unedited tree's on both compilers.
  - **Not measured.** The final form, with D-OUT-2's exception in place of the probe's returned code (an implementation step with a return condition, §5). The compare's cost in time: no benchmark was run for this note; one runs in the verification phase (§5, "Gates"); until then it is reasoned as one compare on a field the function's callers have just written.
- **D-OUT-3's member** is not a coroutine and is called on the failure branch only.
- **D-OUT-9 restructures `Session::send`'s catch**, which a send that succeeds does not reach: its handlers note, and the reset, the state write and the return follow them. `Session::send`'s frame is on the chain W-E counts; whether the change grows it is not measured (the same implementation step).
- **D-OUT-6** is on the close path.

The B&L rows and the cells that point at them take these numbers.

No decision is open in them: the boundary §10.5 shows, a cancellation that reaches `Session::drive_reconnect` before the Logon emission, is fixed in fixpp#585 by ruling R-22.
   - The task list is extended for §9 and §10.
   - `/analyze` over the amendment has run: pass 2, over v0.12 and that task list. Its report and the orchestrator's dispositions are the parent's `research/G19-fix-fpml-iso20022/research/reviews/opus_536_537_538_analyze2_recovery-liveness.md`; the wording items it left for this note are v0.13.

Gate A is the targeted round and the loop that follows it, which converged at review index 8. The owner signed off the converged note, as the plan, on 2026-10-12. `/analyze` over the amendment has run (pass 2; item 4 names its report).
6. **Not checked for v0.9:** QuickFIX/Go on a store write that fails during a send (no local copy); where QuickFIX C++ and QuickFIX/J write the last-sent time their logout timeout counts from (§4.5).

**Where §9 and §10 land, relative to the phases of the task list.** The task list assigns the ids; this is the order and its reasons.

- **§9 lands before Phase 1, as a phase of its own.** The catch is upstream of every arm that Phases 1 to 3 edit, and every cell of those phases that stops an engine or cancels a driver sees its ending. Landing it first means each later cell is written once, against the ending it will keep.
  - Rewritten in that phase: T0A.8's measurement cell and the `B35InboundSplitCancel.*_RunsBeforeTheThrow` cells (§9.5), and the cells of §5's first new table that do not depend on D-538-4.
  - Written in Phase 1, with D-538-4: the returned twin of "the calling thread after the call", which needs the walk to return `store_cancelled`.
- **§10 lands after Phase 2 and before Phase 3, as a phase of its own.** It edits `run_logout_phase1`, which D-536-3 rewrites in Phase 2, so the early return is written once, on the rewritten wait. Phase 3's deadline Logout goes through `inbound_logout_leaf_`, a caller that discards the emit's result; with §10 in place before it, that cell is written against the ending it will keep.
  - Rewritten in that phase: T0A.13's cells, `StoreFailReconcileReadFailTest.StoreCancelled_NotFatal_StaysActiveAndTransmits`, and the lock cell of fixpp#544 (§10.4).
  - The guard in `ensure_hydrated_` (D-CAN-1, R-20) lands with §9, in the function it edits. Its cell needs D-OUT-5's handler, so it is written in §10's phase, with the Logon root's row. Probe 8's cells are written in §10's phase from the probe's source.
- **States that exist only between phases** (the branch carries no gate label in any of them):
  - after §9 and before Phase 1: no exception leaves `on_inbound_frame`, and a cancelled read of the resend walk on the returned timing is still folded into a GapFill;
  - after §9 and before §10: a cancelled outbound store still has two endings on `Session::send`'s path, and on the inbound path its thrown timing ends through the catch (when a later await of the arm meets the cancellation) while its returned timing still transmits;
  - after §10 and before the documents phase: B&L, the catalogue note and the `brain/` row still state 059's D7.

## 8. What this does not decide

- **fixpp#577:** a too-high ResendRequest or Logout is discarded rather than acted on. Unconfirmed. §5's two cells for those frames assert only what survives either answer.
- **fixpp#578:** the awaiting-resend flag across an in-place reconnect. Unconfirmed. If it holds, it interacts with D-537-5: the flag is cleared in `close_filled_resend_gap_` and `apply_inbound_sequence_reset` and set in `on_inbound_active_too_high_` (`git grep -n -E 'enter_awaiting_resend|exit_awaiting_resend' -- src`), and nothing clears it at a reconnect or in the reset unit. The Logon paths and the reset unit would then move NextNumIn with the flag set, and the first too-high frame after a reconnect could meet a time point from the previous connection and draw the "unanswered resend" Logout at once, for a request never sent on that connection. The deadline's own exit leaves the flag set too, as every other end of a session does today. Reasoned; not fixed here.
  - **Who can reach it, read.** Under an `Engine` no `Session` is connected twice: `run_connect_loop` constructs one `Session`, calls `drive_reconnect` once, runs the read pump and returns, and the accept loop constructs a `Session` for each connection it matches (`git grep -n -E 'make_shared<Session>|drive_reconnect\(\)' -- src/session`). So under an `Engine` the flag is never set when a connection starts. Only a `Session` that its own driver reconnects in place, by calling the public `drive_reconnect` again, can reach the interaction.
  - **The condition that can send this design back.** It is not a measurement, so it is here and not in §5's table of implementation steps. No cell reconnects with the flag set. If fixpp#578 is confirmed before this PR merges, D-537-5's interaction with it is re-read.
- **fixpp#534** (B27): `Disconnected` does not close the transport. The deadline's Logout inherits it (D-537-5), and the deadline cells assert the open transport so that B27 flips it. The mismatch endings of D-537-6 and D-537-8 inherit it too: they are silent `Disconnected` writes, like the in-sequence one.
- **fixpp#535** (`test_request_threshold`, B27) lives in the liveness loop. This note does not touch it. While #536 is open, a #534 cell must use one session per clock.
- **B27's surface** is `run_liveness_loop` and the `Disconnected` writers. This note adds a state test and a filter reset before each of the loop's sleeps (D-536-2) and changes no other line of it. D-537-6 and D-537-8 share `pending_test_req_id_` and `unanswered_tr_` with the loop, and no line.
- **The grace exit reads only the pending id**, not "any sound frame since the TestRequest" (the consult's B′). That departs from Session Layer §4.10 precedence 14 for every peer that sends traffic and no Heartbeat, with or without a gap. It is in `run_liveness_loop`. Noticed, not changed (R-6).
- **With `validate_sequence_numbers = false`**, a too-high Heartbeat does not answer a pending TestRequest. Noticed, not changed: D-537-6's gate excludes it, and D-537-8's arm is behind the same knob. A follow-up is to be filed (the "follow-up to file" bullet on this knob, below).
- **A follow-up to file: the per-slot walk's start for BeginSeqNo(7) = 0** (R-10). Today's answer starts with a GapFill whose MsgSeqNum(34) is 0 (D-538-4). Clamping the walk's start to 1, as its empty-store branch does, changes the wire and is not in this batch. Filed as fixpp#580.
- **A follow-up to file: the residual pattern** (R-11, D-537-9). A peer that sends only frames that are not too high and move nothing keeps a session with an unfilled gap Active without end. Filed as fixpp#581.
- **A follow-up, filed as fixpp#582: with `validate_sequence_numbers` off, no out-of-sequence Heartbeat can answer a TestRequest.** Read from `on_inbound_active_out_of_sequence_`: a Heartbeat returns at the function's Heartbeat test, before the knob-off arm, so a Heartbeat whose MsgSeqNum(34) is not exactly the expected number never clears `pending_test_req_id_`, whether it is too high or too low. With the knob off such a frame does not move the expected number, so once the peer's numbers are ahead no later Heartbeat can answer, and a quiet line ends the session at the grace check. It is behaviour at the base, outside the three issues, and not a change to this design. Filed at sign-off.
- **B41** (fixpp#563, #564; its note is on its own branch):
  - B41 edits `Session::send`, `Engine::send`, the callback dispatch scope, and the entry of `Session::close`. It does not touch `run_liveness_loop`, `run_logout_phase1`, `close`'s phase 2, `on_inbound_logout_sent_`, `store_then_emit`, `replay_outbound_range_` or the stores.
  - The only shared function is `Session::close`. The second PR to merge rebases it and re-runs its cells.
  - **v0.9 widens the contact.** §10 relies on `Session::send`'s catch (D-OUT-4): a cancelled store now reaches it as the exception, where it reached the value test below `send_impl`. D-OUT-9 edits that catch: its handlers note, and the reset, the state write and the return follow them. So `Session::send` is a second shared function, beside `Session::close`; the second PR to merge rebases it and re-runs the cells "D-OUT-1, an application's send, both timings" and "D-OUT-9, a directly driven send, both timings".
  - **B41 has withdrawn the breaking C-ABI change it had declared** (§0). This batch's entry is 1.12, with no merge-order condition.
  - B41 tracks callback depth inside the one dispatch routine every `toAdmin` and `toApp` goes through. This note's new emit, the deadline's Logout, goes through the existing inbound Logout leaf, so it is covered with no action here.
  - B41 relies on one premise that D-537-5 states as a condition: at every inbound arm the outbound number is peeked after any inbound callback of that arm. The helper's Logout peeks inside its leaf, and no inbound callback runs between that peek and the leaf's store call at any call site. In the Reset-mode arm the helper follows `fromAdmin`; v0.3's wording ("ahead of every callback of its arm") no longer describes that arm.
  - B41's ruling on `close` makes one interleaving reachable from an application, and D-536-2's state test before the grace sleep covers it (§3.2, §5).
- **The lock sequence's terminal reset as a general hazard** (§3.5). This note fixes the one member that this change exposes. Whether the lock should restore the caller's filter instead is a `2f` design question and is not decided here. Filed as fixpp#583 (batch B45).
  - **These statements of v0.9 depend on what a lock leaves on its caller's thread, and B45 must keep them aligned.** D-CAN-3: after a caught cancellation the inbound entry leaves the thread as a cancelled lock wait leaves it; the pair cell "the calling thread after the call" (§5) compares the two timings and turns RED if one side alone changes. D-OUT-9: the same statement after `Session::send`'s catch, with its own pair cell. D-OUT-5: the same statement after the handler around `emit_initiator_logon_`'s body. If B45 changes what a lock leaves, all three are removed or re-aligned with it.
- **fixpp#584 (batch B46, after B28 and B41 merge): a cancellation met in a reply's transmit step** (R-18; D-CAN-6, case 2). It ends in more than one way by timing, at the base and after this batch. It is to be fixed there, not disclosed; §9 states the routes and the candidate the issue records. The cell "D-CAN-6, case 2, the transmit step as it is" pins what that batch flips.
- **What §9 leaves outside R-14, case 1** (D-CAN-6): a cancellation recorded when the caller awaits `on_inbound_frame`. Under an `Engine` it ends as every `Engine::stop` that cancels a parked read ends, with the pump ending by exception and the stop closing the session. Not changed.
- **`logout_seen_` is set for a Logout that was not sent** (D-OUT-6), so `reset_on_logout` resets the sequence numbers after a graceful close whose Logout never reached the peer. Behaviour that exists today; R-17 does not rule on it. Noticed, not changed.
- **A cancellation that lands on 059's reconcile read** (D-OUT-2, "Unchanged"). The frame's ending is 059's, and the number can stay one ahead in memory (`B-059-1`, "Residual window"). Not changed.
- **A `Session` reconnected in place after a cancelled outbound store** (D-OUT-3). What it sends next is reasoned; no fixture reconnects a `Session` in place. It is the class of fixpp#578.
- **fixpp#585 (batch B47, after B28 merges): a cancellation that reaches `Session::drive_reconnect` before the Logon is sent** (R-22; §10.5). It ends as a returned error or as a thrown exception, by timing, at the base and after this batch, and after a successful connect it leaves the session `LogonSent` with a live transport. It is to be fixed there, not disclosed; §10.5 states the routes.
- **An inbound arm that is already running when the session ends** may still send its remaining replies while the transport is open (D-OUT-10). Behaviour that exists today, for every writer of `Disconnected`; it is the class of fixpp#534. Not changed; one cell pins it (§5). With a second send refused by a `FileStore` at the same moment, queued behind the cancelled one (or behind one whose write failed; D-OUT-10), those replies go out or not by the order in which that send and the arm's inbound persist reached the store's lock, and in one order a reply stays in the store unsent (D-OUT-10; measured, Probe 9a); R-23 settles it with this class, and its cell of §5 names the assertion that issue's batch flips.
- **A session with neither a live transport nor a callback sink** keeps the logout wait (D-OUT-6). A directly driven `Session` only. Not changed.
- Noticed and left alone:
  - `tls_transport.hpp` says its handshake timeout uses `Clock::sleep_until`, and the code uses a private timer;
  - `reconnect_fsm.hpp` declares three timers nothing reads (fixpp#559's class);
  - `FileStoreImpl::write_frame` has no caller;
  - `run_liveness_loop` is off only at a HeartBtInt of exactly 0. With a negative interval its deadlines are already past, so its first pass sends a Heartbeat and a TestRequest and ends the session at the grace check. Read, not run;
  - `pending_test_req_id_` is written by the liveness loop's assign and by the in-sequence Heartbeat's clear, and by nothing else (the recipe is in D-537-6). Nothing clears it when a session ends. What an id left from an earlier connection does after an in-place reconnect was not traced; it is the class of fixpp#578;
  - when the peer's Logout arrives while fixpp's own Logout emit is suspended, `run_logout_phase1` records `LogoutSent` over `Disconnected` and waits for its deadline although the peer has logged out (the trace is in §5's D-536-3 control). Behaviour that exists today;
  - `FileStore::store` awaits its offload inside a `try` whose handler reads every `operation_aborted` as "the write completed durably". The `co_await` of the offload is pre-checked like any other, so a cancellation recorded on the thread between the store's lock and that `co_await` would be read as a completed write that never started. No suspension separates the two statements, so on one serialisation domain nothing can record one there. Read and reasoned, not run; not changed.

## 9. A cancellation met while an inbound frame is processed (R-14)

This section's review rounds and their dispositions are logged under `## Gate A`; the owner signed off this loop on 2026-10-12. Words used here: the *thread* is the asio coroutine thread that awaits `Session::on_inbound_frame` (under an `Engine`, the role loop's, through the read pump). A cancellation is *recorded* when an emit on that thread's slot passed its filter. The *returned* timing is the one in which an awaited call takes the cancellation while it is suspended and reports it as an error value. The *thrown* timing is the one in which the cancellation is already recorded when a `co_await` is evaluated: asio's pre-check then throws `operation_aborted` in the awaiting frame, before the awaited call's body starts.

**For the owner at sign-off.** Item 0 is ruled (R-18). The rulings do not decide items 1 to 5; this section does, and each can be overturned without touching the others.

0. **Ruled by R-18: a cancellation met in a reply's transmit step is fixed in its own batch** (fixpp#584, B46, after B28 and B41 merge). It has more than one route by timing and the routes do not end the same way; this section changes nothing in that step and states the boundary (D-CAN-6, case 2).
1. **The code.** The catch returns `store_cancelled` for a cancellation, not `Session::send`'s `dispatch_aborted` (D-CAN-4). R-14's words name no code. R-13's do ("the session ends with `store_cancelled`"), for the returned timing of a cancelled read, and one catch cannot tell which await threw; so the code that makes both timings of a cancelled read agree is R-13's. Choosing `dispatch_aborted` instead would need R-13's and R-7's quoted code reworded for a cancelled read, and the returned codes of the hydration reads, the fatal reset and the inbound persist changed.
2. **One store call's returned code changes.** `persist_inbound_advance_` returns `store_cancelled` when the store returned it, where it returns `store_io_failure` for every failed store call today (D-CAN-4). Without it the two timings of a cancelled inbound persist return two codes.
3. **A Logon arm that a close or an engine stop has superseded, and that met a cancellation, records nothing and returns success, on both timings** (D-CAN-5). On the returned timing that changes what the store calls of the Logon arms that return their error return today when superseded (D-CAN-5 names them): the store's code becomes success.
4. **What R-14 does not cover** (D-CAN-6, case 1): a cancellation recorded when the caller awaits `on_inbound_frame`. It does not throw out of `on_inbound_frame`, and the session has not started the frame.
5. **The reset** clears, at the inbound entry, a cancellation that belongs to the caller of `on_inbound_frame` (D-CAN-3). The returned timing already does so, through the store's lock (fixpp#583), and `Session::close` does so for its caller.

### 9.1 What happens today

- **Traced by reading (T0A.7).** No function between a `co_await` of the inbound path and the role loop's `co_spawn` holds a `try` that covers it. The exception leaves `on_inbound_frame`, the pump and the role loop, and asio's `detached` handler drops it. Recipe: list `try` and `catch (` in `src/session/session.cpp` and `src/session/engine.cpp`, and name the function that holds each. The ones on the inbound path are in `live_write_serialized_` (around the write gate and around the write), in `store_then_emit` (around the store call, and around the callback transport's send), and in the walk's `transmit_async` (around the callback transport's send).
- **Measured (T0A.8; Probe 2, cells `Thrown_Pump` and `Thrown_Direct`, unedited tree).** `Engine::stop` is spawned in the walk's call expression of a slot's `retrieve`, so its cancellation is recorded when the walk awaits that read. The read's body never starts. `on_inbound_frame` throws `operation_aborted` to a direct caller. Under an `Engine`, when the handler in which that happened has returned: the state is `Active`, `onLogout` has not fired, and the peer sees no hang-up; the state is `Disconnected` and `onLogout` has fired once `Engine::stop` has completed. The peer reads the frame replayed before the read and then end-of-stream.
- **The returned timing, measured on the unedited tree (Probe 2, cells `Returned_Pump` and `Returned_Direct`).** The scene is an in-sequence Heartbeat whose inbound persist is queued at a `FileStore`'s lock when `Engine::stop` is spawned. The persist returns `store_cancelled`; the arm records `Disconnected` and returns an error; `onLogout` has fired and the peer sees a hang-up when that handler has returned; the calling thread is not cancelled; the read pump's own `close(terminal)` has drained the session before `Engine::stop` reaches its own close.
- **So one cancellation has two endings by timing.** They differ in when `Disconnected` is recorded and `onLogout` fires, in which coroutine runs the session's close, and, for a direct caller, in whether `on_inbound_frame` returns or throws and in what its thread's cancellation state is afterwards.
- **It is not particular to the walk.** The pre-check guards every `co_await` of an awaitable and of an asio operation. §9.2 lists the kinds on the inbound path.
- **A worse case, read and not run.** An exception that is not a cancellation leaves the same way: one that a store's coroutine body lets out of `retrieve`, `next_seqnum`, `reset` or `reset_to` (a third-party store), or `std::bad_alloc` from a callee that is not `noexcept`. Under an `Engine` the pump then ends by exception with the session still `Active` and the transport open, and nothing closes either until the peer closes or `Engine::stop` runs.

### 9.2 Every kind of await on the inbound path, and both timings of a cancellation at it

- **How it was derived.** `command grep -n -E 'co_await|FIXPP_INBOUND_SPLIT' src/session/session.cpp`, each hit attributed to the function that holds it, then walked from `on_inbound_active_` through every arm, sub-arm and reply leaf it reaches. Re-derive it the same way; do not extend the table from a symptom.
- **The split boundaries add no kind.** `FIXPP_INBOUND_SPLIT_AWAIT` clears the thread's throw-if-cancelled flag for the one `co_await` of the callee and the callee's `FIXPP_INBOUND_SPLIT_ENTRY` restores it, so the first pre-check after a boundary is the callee's first own `co_await`. Read from the two macros.
- **"After Phase 1"** is the tree with D-538-4 and without §9.

| Kind of await | Sites | Returned timing: is there one, and what the arm does | Thrown timing today | Both timings after §9 and §10 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A store read of the resend walk | `replay_outbound_range_`: the tail read and each slot's `retrieve` | yes, when the call queues at the store's lock. Today the result is folded and the walk goes on. After Phase 1: the walk stops, its caller records `Disconnected`, `on_inbound_frame` returns `store_cancelled` (R-13) | an exception out of `on_inbound_frame`; the session stays `Active` | `Disconnected` and `store_cancelled`; for a superseded Logon arm (the 789 path), nothing recorded and success (D-CAN-5); no exception; the thread not cancelled |
| The inbound persist | `persist_inbound_advance_`: `next_seqnum(inbound, true)` | yes, on a persistent store. `Disconnected`, unless a Logon arm is superseded; returns `store_io_failure`, whatever the store returned | as above | `Disconnected` and `store_cancelled` (D-CAN-4); for a superseded Logon arm, nothing recorded and success (D-CAN-5); no exception; the thread not cancelled |
| The hydration reads | `ensure_hydrated_`: its `next_seqnum(·, false)` reads, in the acceptor's Logon arm. The initiator reaches the same reads from `emit_initiator_logon_`, which is not on the inbound path: D-OUT-5 and §10.5 | yes, on a persistent store. `Disconnected` unless superseded; returns the store's code | as above; the state stays what the arm had | as the inbound persist |
| The reset of `reset_on_logon` | `reset_seqnums_to_one_durable(fatal)`: the store's `reset()`, in the acceptor's Logon arm | yes. `Disconnected` unless superseded; returns the store's code | as above | as the hydration reads |
| The outbound store of a reply | `store_then_emit`, from every reply leaf and helper of §10's table | yes. Today the result is absorbed and the reply is transmitted | caught in `store_then_emit`, which returns `dispatch_aborted` with the thread still cancelled; a caller that discards the result goes on to its next await, which throws | §10: the reply is not transmitted, the number is taken back, and `store_then_emit` leaves by the exception on both timings (D-OUT-2); the catch of D-CAN-1 then gives the row above |
| The write gate and the transport write (a reply's transmit step) | `live_write_serialized_`, from `store_then_emit` and from the walk's `transmit_async` | yes, by more than one route. A cancelled wait for the write gate: the lock reports it and leaves the thread clear. A cancelled write in flight: the transport reports it, and the cancellation stays recorded, because the transport resets the state at its entry and not at its exit. On both, `store_then_emit` returns `dispatch_aborted` | at the `co_await` of `live_write_serialized_` itself, in `store_then_emit`'s frame or in the walk's lambda: thrown, outside any `try`, so an exception out of `on_inbound_frame`. At an await inside `live_write_serialized_`: caught by its own `try`, the same `dispatch_aborted`, the thread still cancelled | the first of those is R-14's class and ends at D-CAN-1's catch. The rest is **unchanged by §9, and not one ending** (D-CAN-6, case 2; fixed in fixpp#584 by ruling R-18). The value `store_then_emit` returns is one; what follows it depends on the route and on the caller |
| A sequence-number lock | `assign_outbound`, `check_inbound`, `set_next_inbound`, `set_next_outbound`, `reset_to_one`, `hydrate` | no, on one serialisation domain: no critical section of `SeqnumManager` suspends, so a wait for its lock never queues. A drained mutex returns `session_already_closed`, which is the session's close and not a cancellation | an exception out of `on_inbound_frame` | the catch's ending: `Disconnected` and `store_cancelled`; for a superseded Logon arm, nothing recorded and success; no exception; the thread not cancelled |
| A session coroutine awaited directly | every arm's `co_await` of a member coroutine that is not behind a split boundary; the walk's two lambdas | no: the pre-check is the only place a cancellation is met at such an await | as above | as above |
| `Session::close(terminal)` awaited by an arm | the arms that close on a late parse failure or a throwing callback | no: `close` disables cancellation as its first statement, inside its body | the pre-check throws before `close` is entered | as above. The close the arm meant to run is then the pump's, or the driver's |
| The reset unit's store operation | `run_reset_unit_`: a `co_spawn` bound to an empty cancellation slot | no: the empty slot keeps every emit out of the store operation | the pre-check of the `co_await` of the spawn throws before the operation is started. Reasoned from asio's `await_transform`; not run | as above |
| Not pre-checked | `this_coro::executor`, `this_coro::reset_cancellation_state`, the split macros' flag reads | — | never throws | — |

- **Read off the table.** Outside the transmit step, a cancellation has both timings only at a store call; at every other kind it is met by the pre-check alone. The transmit step has both timings, and a third route, and is D-CAN-6's case 2.
- **Conditions.** "No critical section of `SeqnumManager` suspends" was read from every body of `src/session/seqnum_manager.cpp` that takes the manager's lock: none has a `co_await` between its lock and its return. An executor that runs one session's coroutines on more than one thread at a time is outside this table; no shipped driver does that.
- **When a cancellation can be recorded on the thread while a frame is processed**, read: after a transport write that accepted it and returned (the write resets the state to accept a total cancellation and does not reset again); and after each `reset_cancellation_state(enable_total_cancellation{})` statement that follows a reset unit in the Logon arms. After any lock on the thread the filter is terminal (§3.5), so `Engine::stop`'s total emit is then dropped until one of those resets.

### 9.3 Decisions

- **D-CAN-1 (R-14). One `try` around the whole body of `Session::on_inbound_active_`.**
  - It is the single coroutine every inbound frame enters, in every state: it hands a frame for a cold state to `on_inbound_cold_` behind a split boundary, inside the same body. So one site covers the Logon arms, `LogoutSent` and `Disconnected` too. Read.
  - `on_inbound_frame` stays the plain function that fixpp#544 (B35, its ruling A → a2) made it; it adds no coroutine frame.
  - The split macros compile inside the `try` as they are, on gcc 13 and on clang (measured, Probe 1 and Probe 2: the candidate built on both).
  - **One member the unwinding would leave set is cleared on every exit (R-20; the round's O-3).** `ensure_hydrated_` sets `hydrating_` before its first read and clears it at each `co_return`; an exception that leaves the function skips the clear, and a later `ensure_hydrated_` on the same `Session` then returns at its re-entrancy test without reading the store. The function clears the flag on every exit, by a guard object in its body, so the two timings of a cancelled hydration read leave it the same. On the inbound path nothing reads the flag again after the session has ended; on the initiator's path a second `drive_reconnect` does (D-OUT-5). Recipe for other members of that kind: on each path from a `co_await` to the catch, list the statements between a member write and the write that undoes it; the triage of the round read them and found this one, the frame's inbound persist (skipped, as for every failing reply today) and the callbacks of D-OUT-1's row.
- **D-CAN-2 (R-14). The handler takes every exception.** That is the shape of `Session::send`'s catch, whose handlers all have one disposition.
  - **Why this is inside R-14 and not an extension.** R-14's ending is "no exception out of on_inbound_frame". A handler for `operation_aborted` alone would leave §9.1's worse case as it is.
  - **The handler only notes which kind it caught**, in a local: a cancellation (`asio::system_error` whose code is `operation_aborted`) or anything else. A `co_await` may not appear in a handler, so everything else happens after it.
  - **The ending of an exception that is not a cancellation:** `Disconnected` is recorded, unless D-CAN-5's predicate holds, when nothing is recorded; `on_inbound_frame` returns `dispatch_aborted` in both cases, the code `Session::send`'s catch returns; nothing is thrown. D-CAN-3's reset runs for it too: the thread then has the terminal filter any lock on the path would have left. Under an `Engine`, for a frame the pump delivered, the pump's failed-delivery branch then closes the session and the transport at once. The acceptor's first Logon is delivered by `run_accept_loop` itself, which discards the result and starts the pump (D-538-4, "Where this ending closes the transport"): the pump then runs on a session that is `Disconnected`, and closes it at the establishment deadline or when its read ends.
- **D-CAN-3 (R-14). After the handler, the thread's cancellation state is the one the returned timing leaves.**
  - **The requirement, as a condition.** When `on_inbound_frame` returns after a caught exception, no cancellation is recorded on the calling thread and its filter is the terminal one. That is the state a lock sequence leaves after a cancelled wait (`FIXPP_DETAIL_CO_AWAIT_LOCK`, part 4), which is where the returned timing gets it.
  - **How it is met.** The first statement after the handler is `co_await asio::this_coro::reset_cancellation_state()`, with no argument: it replaces the thread's state with a fresh one under asio's default filter. It is never pre-checked and cannot throw. Read from asio's `awaitable.hpp`.
  - **Why it is needed (measured, Probe 2).** The catch without the reset meets "no exception out of on_inbound_frame" and nothing more. The cancellation is still recorded, so the pump's next `co_await`, of its own `close(terminal)`, throws; the pump ends by exception as today, and `Engine::stop` closes the transport and the session. A direct caller's next `co_await` throws too. With the reset, the thrown timing reads the same as the returned one on every observation of Probe 2's tables except the returned code, which D-CAN-4 settles.
  - **The precedent.** `Session::close` replaces its caller's thread state with `disable_cancellation` and does not restore it (§3.5).
  - **For a `Session` driven without an `Engine`.** A cancellation the driver emitted on its own slot, and which a pre-check of the frame's processing met, is no longer recorded when `on_inbound_frame` returns: the driver's later awaits do not throw for it. The driver learns of it from the returned code. That is what the returned timing does today.
  - **The dependency on fixpp#583 (B45).** The returned timing leaves the thread clear only because the lock sequence resets the state at its end, which is the behaviour fixpp#583 questions. If B45 changes what a lock leaves on its caller's thread, the statement of this decision must change with it, so that the two timings stay equal. The pair of cells "the calling thread after the call" (§5) is the instrument: it compares the two timings and names no absolute state, so a change on one side alone turns it RED.
- **D-CAN-4 (R-14). One code for both timings of a cancellation at a store call; the transmit step is fixpp#584's.**
  - **The catch returns `store_cancelled` for a cancellation.** The derivation: §9.2 shows that, outside the transmit step, both timings exist only at store calls; R-13 fixes the returned timing's code for the walk's reads as `store_cancelled`, and the hydration reads and the fatal reset already return the store's own code; one catch at the entry cannot tell which await threw; so the one code it can return that agrees with the returned timing at every store call is `store_cancelled`.
  - **`persist_inbound_advance_` returns `store_cancelled` unchanged when the store returned it.** For every other failed store call it keeps `store_io_failure`. It is the one returned code this section changes.
  - **At an await that is not a store call** (a sequence-number lock, a member coroutine, `close`, the reset unit) the name is wider than the event: no store was cancelled. That kind has one timing, so no second code exists to disagree with. `error.hpp`'s comment on `store_cancelled` gains the sentence that says so (§6.4).
  - **Scope, row by row of §9.2's table.** Every row of that table is in one of the groups below; none is left with two codes and no owner.
    - *Made equal by this decision, both timings returning `store_cancelled`:* a store read of the resend walk (R-13 gives the returned code); the inbound persist (by the pass-through above); the hydration reads and the reset of `reset_on_logon` (each returns the store's own code today); the outbound store of a reply (both timings reach the catch, D-OUT-2). **Condition:** the store reports a call that a cancellation ended as `store_cancelled`. Both shipped stores do, at each failed lock of each method (read); `.specify/2e-msgstore.md`'s cancellation table asks it of every method, and D-OUT-7's sentence puts it into `message_store.hpp`. A third-party store that reports a cancelled read by another code gets that code on the returned timing and `store_cancelled` on the thrown one; that store does not conform.
    - *One timing only, so one code:* a sequence-number lock (condition: one serialisation domain, §9.2); a session coroutine awaited directly, `ReconnectFsm::enter_awaiting_resend` among them (its body has no await); `Session::close(terminal)` awaited by an arm; the reset unit's store operation. The catch's `store_cancelled` is their only code.
    - *Not made equal, and owned by fixpp#584 (R-18):* the transmit step, that is, the write gate and the transport write. Route (w0) ends at the catch with `store_cancelled`; routes (w1) to (w3) return `dispatch_aborted` from `store_then_emit`, and what `on_inbound_frame` then returns depends on the caller (D-CAN-6, case 2).
    - *Never throws:* the row "not pre-checked".
  - **Who reads the code.** Under an `Engine`, nothing: the pump tests only whether the result holds a value, and no C function receives it. The one observer is a C++ driver of a `Session`.
  - Not taken: `dispatch_aborted` from the catch (item 1 of the block above).
- **D-CAN-5 (R-14). A Logon arm that a close or an engine stop has superseded, and that met a cancellation, records nothing and returns success, on both timings.**
  - **The returned timing today, read.** In the Logon arms (`on_inbound_not_connected_`, `on_inbound_logon_sent_`) and the helpers they call, every state write that follows a suspension goes through `logon_arm_superseded_` or `disconnect_unless_superseded_`: when a close is in flight, or `Engine::stop` has set the session's engine-stop flag, or the state is no longer the one the arm expects, nothing is recorded. `Engine::stop` sets the flag before it emits. Recipe: `git grep -n -E 'logon_arm_superseded_\(|disconnect_unless_superseded_\(' -- src/session/session.cpp`, and name the function that holds each hit.
  - **What a superseded arm returns today depends on the site.** Sites that report `superseded` return success: `honor_peer_next_expected_`, and `store_then_emit` for a caller that passed its arm. Sites that call `disconnect_unless_superseded_` and then return their error return that error with nothing recorded: among the store calls, the hydration reads, the arm's inbound persist and the fatal reset. So today the returned timing has no single value under supersession, and a catch alone could not match both groups.
  - **The rule, as one predicate used at each of the statements below, both in `on_inbound_active_`.** The function keeps the state it read at its entry. *Superseded* is: that state was `NotConnected` or `LogonSent`, and the session's lifecycle is no longer open (a close is in flight or has completed) or the engine-stop flag is set.
    - **After the handler** (the thrown timing): when the predicate holds, nothing is recorded; success is returned for a cancellation, and `dispatch_aborted` for any other exception, as the sites that return their error with nothing recorded do. When it does not hold, `Disconnected` is recorded, if the state is not already `Disconnected`, and D-CAN-2's or D-CAN-4's code is returned.
    - **At the return of the cold dispatch** (the returned timing): both Logon arms are cold arms, and `on_inbound_active_` awaits them at one statement, at its top, and returns their result there. When that result is `store_cancelled` and the predicate holds, success is returned in its place. The arm has recorded nothing, by the code that exists today.
  - **So under supersession both timings of a cancelled store call in a Logon arm read the same:** nothing recorded, success. With no supersession both read `Disconnected` and `store_cancelled` (D-CAN-4). The second statement is on the cold branch, not on the path of a frame in `Active`.
  - **Why success.** It is what the arm returns today when `honor_peer_next_expected_` reports `superseded`, and what `store_then_emit` returns for a superseded arm. The session then ends through the close or the stop that superseded it. Under an `Engine` the pump goes on to its next read, which the stop's or the close's transport close ends, and runs the session's close there.
  - **What the returned timing changes.** At the store calls named above, a superseded arm whose store call was cancelled returns success where it returns the store's code today (`store_io_failure` for the persist). An error that is not a cancellation is returned as today.
  - **Where the predicate differs from `logon_arm_superseded_`, and where that matters.**
    - It also holds when a close has completed, not only while one is in flight. `logon_arm_superseded_` reaches that case through its third leg (the state is no longer the one the arm expects), which the entry cannot test: it does not hold the arm's expected state, which changes as the arm advances.
    - It does not test that third leg. The leg alone holds only when something other than a close or an engine stop wrote the state while the arm was suspended. For an arm that has not reached `Active`, the writers of the state outside the arm are `Session::close` and a second inbound frame delivered while the first is suspended; no shipped driver delivers the second (§5's LogonReceived waiver gives the reading). For an arm that has reached `Active`, the liveness loop and `Session::send` are writers too, and the arm's one remaining store call is its inbound persist, which returns its error on the returned timing and D-CAN-4's code on the thrown one: the same value, with the state left as the other writer set it. Read and reasoned; the condition is "no driver delivers two frames to one session at a time".
  - **The frame is still a Logon frame when the arm has reached `Active`.** The test is on the state at entry, not on the current one: the last awaits of a Logon arm run with the session already `Active`.
- **D-CAN-6 (R-14). What R-14 covers: an exception that would leave the processing of a frame the session has started.** The cases below are outside it.
  - **Case 1: a cancellation recorded when the caller awaits `on_inbound_frame`.** The pre-check runs in the caller's frame and throws there; `on_inbound_active_` is never entered, and no catch inside the session can see it. Under an `Engine` the window is between a read's completion with data and the pump's delivery, and between two frames of one read.
    - **It is not a second ending of R-14's condition.** The session has not started the frame: NextNumIn has not moved, no callback ran, nothing was sent. It is the ending of every `Engine::stop` that cancels a parked read: the read returns its cancelled code with the cancellation still recorded, the pump's `co_await` of its own close throws, the pump ends by exception, and `Engine::stop` closes the transport and the session. `Engine::stop`'s comment at its step 4 says so, and `Session::close`'s comment refers to it.
    - Not measured (Probe 2, "Not measured"): no gate places an emit in that window, and whether a handler can run there on one strand is not established.
  - **Case 2: a cancellation met in a reply's transmit step. Outside this batch by ruling (R-18): fixed in fixpp#584, batch B46.** Except on route (w0) it throws nothing out of `on_inbound_frame`, so R-14's words do not reach it, and §9 does not change it. What follows is the boundary R-18 asks this note to state: R-14 covers exceptions leaving the inbound entry, R-15 the store step, and the transmit step keeps the routes below until fixpp#584 fixes them.
    - **The routes, read from `store_then_emit`, the walk's `transmit_async`, `live_write_serialized_`, the lock sequence and the plain transport.** On each of them the frame is in the store and its number is consumed.
      - (w0) The cancellation is already recorded when the transmit step is awaited, for instance one recorded during the store call after the store's lock was taken (reasoned from `FileStore::store`, which reports a write as done whatever was recorded during its offload). The pre-check of the `co_await` of `live_write_serialized_` throws in the awaiting frame, outside any `try`. This route already leaves `store_then_emit` by an exception today. On the inbound path it is R-14's class, and after §9 it ends at D-CAN-1's catch.
      - (w1) The wait for the write gate is cancelled: the lock reports it, and its sequence leaves the thread clear. `store_then_emit` returns `dispatch_aborted`.
      - (w2) The write in flight is cancelled: the transport reports it, and the cancellation stays recorded on the thread. `store_then_emit` returns `dispatch_aborted`.
      - (w3) A pre-check inside `live_write_serialized_`, at the gate or at the write, throws into that function's own `try`: `dispatch_aborted`, the cancellation still recorded. No suspension precedes the gate's await in that function, so on one serialisation domain this route needs a gate wait that queued and was granted; whether it is reachable was not established.
    - **What follows, by caller** (D-OUT-4's table says which callers return the emit's result and which discard it).
      - On (w0), every caller: D-CAN-1's ending after §9 (`Disconnected`, `store_cancelled`, the thread clear).
      - A caller that returns the emit's result records `Disconnected`, and `on_inbound_frame` returns `dispatch_aborted`. On (w1) the calling thread is clear, and under an `Engine` the pump runs the session's close. On (w2) and (w3) it is still cancelled: the pump's `co_await` of its close throws, the pump ends by exception, and `Engine::stop` runs the close (case 1's ending).
      - A caller that discards it goes on. On (w1) the arm continues as after any failed write, and a later reply of the same arm can be sent. On (w2) and (w3) the arm's next await throws, which D-CAN-1's catch ends: `Disconnected`, `store_cancelled`, the thread clear. If the arm has no further await, `on_inbound_frame` returns the arm's own value with the cancellation still recorded.
    - **So one cancellation of one reply's transmit ends in more than one way by timing**, in the returned code, in the calling thread's state, in which coroutine runs the session's close and, for a discarding caller on (w1), in whether a later reply goes out. All of it exists at the base, and fixpp#584 owns it.
    - **The candidate fixpp#584 records.** It is not a decision of this note.
      - Extend D-OUT-2's rule: a cancellation met in `store_then_emit`'s transmit step leaves the function by the exception, as one met in its store step does and as route (w0) already does, with no take-back (the frame is stored). `live_write_serialized_` then reports a cancellation, on each of those routes, apart from a write that failed; today it folds both into one code. Every root of D-OUT-4 already has its handler; on the inbound path the ending is D-CAN-1's on every route. Cost: the cells that assert `dispatch_aborted` from `on_inbound_frame` after a cancelled write change (found by running); `live_write_serialized_` is on the send path that fixpp#544's gates watch; `Session::send`'s result for a cancelled write stays `dispatch_aborted` through its catch (spec 009's scenario); no probe has built it.
    - **An arm with no await after the point where a cancellation was recorded** is the residue of this case and of case 1: `on_inbound_frame` returns the arm's value, and the caller's next await is case 1. Existing cells pin that shape: `B35InboundSplitCancel.LogonSentLogoutBuildFailure_DisconnectsWithoutThrow` and `B35InboundSplitCancel.TooHighResendRequestBuildFailure_StaysActiveWithoutThrow`, and both keep their verdict under the candidate (measured, Probe 2).
    - Not taken in this batch, as a partial remedy: resetting the thread's state on every failing return of `on_inbound_active_`. It would align the calling thread's state on (w2) and (w3) for a caller that returns the result, and leave the returned code and a discarding caller on (w1) as they are. It was not measured.
- **D-CAN-7. The comments that describe the old route are corrected**, not replaced by new claims:
  - `run_read_pump`'s header comment, the "total-cancel (stop())" line of its "Termination" list. It says the read's error arm closes the session and the pump unwinds cleanly. `Engine::stop`'s step 4 comment and `Session::close`'s say the opposite, and they are the ones the code follows (D-CAN-6, case 1).
  - `on_inbound_frame`'s declaration comment in `session.hpp` gains what the function returns for a caught exception, and that it does not throw.
  - `on_inbound_active_` carries a header comment naming this note's ruling (R-14), as the project's instructions ask when a decision about a file is superseded: the cells of fixpp#544's b3 ruling pinned, as a throw out of the frame, the route this `try` now ends.
  - Noticed, not changed: the comment in `Engine::send`'s session-strand lambda says its reset lets `Engine::stop`'s cancellation reach `Session::send`. Nothing emits on a send's thread (T0A.12, read). It is on B41's surface (§8).

### 9.4 Rejected

- **`on_inbound_frame` made a coroutine, with the `try` in it.** It adds a frame cycled on every inbound frame, so the Active chain is one frame deeper than fixpp#544's target, and it reverses that note's ruling A → a2. No gate counts the production chain's depth (W-D-W models it with a replica pair), so the gates would not report it.
- **A `try` at each production caller** (two in `run_read_pump`, one in `run_accept_loop`). `on_inbound_frame` would still throw to every other caller, which R-14's words exclude. A handler in the pump could not await the pump's close either.
- **A handler for `operation_aborted` only.** It leaves §9.1's worse case (D-CAN-2).
- **The catch without the reset.** Measured (Probe 2, candidate N): it changes the state and `onLogout` at the cancellation and nothing else; the pump still ends by exception.
- **`throw_if_cancelled(false)` after the handler, in place of the reset.** It would leave the cancellation recorded and silence every later pre-check on the thread, the caller's included.
- **A conversion at each store call** (a `try` around each `co_await` of a store method that turns the thrown timing into the store's `store_cancelled` and resets the thread there). It would make the two timings run the same statements at every store call, with no test at the entry for a superseded arm. Not taken: it is a `try` at every store call of the session, one of them in `persist_inbound_advance_`, which is cycled on every in-sequence frame and which no probe measured; it would report an exception from inside a third-party store's body as "nothing was read"; and R-14's option is one catch at the entry.
- **`dispatch_aborted` from the catch** (D-CAN-4).

### 9.5 Existing cells whose verdict changes

- **Measured (Probe 2): a named set of binaries was run whole under the candidate, not the suite.** They are the binaries of `test_538_cancelled_read_measured_path.cpp`, `test_cancelled_outbound_store_measurement.cpp`, the reproduction files of §1, `test_544_inbound_split_cancel_cells.cpp` and `engine_reset_unit_stop_test.cpp`. The candidate returned `dispatch_aborted` and had no rule for a superseded arm; D-CAN-4 and D-CAN-5 differ from it there, so the assertions below are re-read against the final form when the cells are rewritten.
- **`CancelledReadMeasuredPath.Measurement_StopAtNextSlotRead_WalkEndsByException`** (T0A.8). It asserts `Active` when the hook's handler has returned. Predicted by D-CAN-1: `Disconnected` there. It is rewritten as the RED-first cell of §5, and its instrument is replaced with it: its transport reading is a zero-wait `poll` for `POLLRDHUP`, which on this host does not report a close made in the same handler (measured, the probes record's "Two findings about instruments"). The rewritten cell uses the waiting form, with the io_context not run during the wait. Its control, `Measurement_Control_HookWithoutStop_ReadEntersTheStore`, keeps its verdict.
- **The `B35InboundSplitCancel.*_RunsBeforeTheThrow` cells** (`SendingTimeGuardLogout_ToAdmin…`, `PossDupArmDLogout_ToAdmin…`, `TooHighResendRequest_ToAdmin…`, `ConfirmingLogout_ToAdmin…`, `TestRequestHeartbeatReply_ToAdmin…`, `BusinessMessageReject_ToApp…`; re-derive with `--gtest_list_tests` on the binary). Each cancels the chain just before a reply block. Its assertions fall into two groups.
  - Those that pin the old ending, and change: "the reply block's first co_await must throw operation_aborted", and the state `Active` after it. Predicted by D-CAN-1 and D-CAN-4: the frame returns `store_cancelled`, throws nothing, and the state is `Disconnected`.
  - Those that fixpp#544 wrote the cells to protect, which must still be asserted: the callback before the block's first original `co_await` runs exactly once, and the reply is not transmitted. They hold under the candidate (measured). They are what shows that a split boundary does not move the first cancellation check ahead of the callback.
  - The cells' own mutant still separates: with the split macros reduced to a plain `co_await`, the check moves to the boundary, ahead of the callback, and the callback count is the assertion that fails. Reasoned from the macros; the mutant is re-run when the cells are edited.
- **The rest of the suite: found by running.** The recipe for candidates is `git grep -l 'on_inbound_frame' -- tests bench`, and in those files `git grep -n 'operation_aborted'`. Its hits outside the split cells' file were read: `coverage_adversarial_test.cpp` (a logout wait's sleep), `send_path_test.cpp` (a store that throws from its `store()`, under `Session::send`), `test_reconnect_live_happy_path.cpp` (the connect path's `drive_reconnect_attempt`), `test_store_fail_reconcile_readfail.cpp` (a comment) and `tests/support/pump_until_ready.hpp` (a comment on a clock sweep). None asserts an exception out of `on_inbound_frame`. T0A.8's own file is not among the hits: it reads the exception through the wrapper's record and does not name it. The recipe finds a file only when it names the exception, so the instrument for the suite is the phase's full `ctest`, compared by name with the baseline.

## 10. A cancelled outbound store, and the graceful Logout that was not sent (R-15, R-16, R-17)

This section's review rounds and their dispositions are logged under `## Gate A`; the owner signed off this loop on 2026-10-12. "Returned" and "thrown" are §9's two timings, at the one `co_await store_->store(…, outbound)` of `Session::store_then_emit`. This section supersedes decision D7 of feature 059 (`specs/059-outbound-store-fail-closed/research.md`), and that feature's D5 and D3 for the cancelled store only.

**For the owner at sign-off.** The rulings do not decide these; this section does, except items 12 and 13, which R-22 and R-21 rule. Items 6 to 12 were added by v0.10, and item 13 by v0.11.

1. **The mechanism is an exception.** On a cancelled store, `store_then_emit` does not return: it leaves by `operation_aborted`, on both timings (D-OUT-2). A returned code can be discarded by a caller, and D-OUT-4's table marks each caller that discards it today.
2. **`store_then_emit` records `Disconnected` itself** for this ending (D-OUT-2), where 059's D3 leaves every transition to the caller.
3. **Two codes, by path.** `Session::send` returns `dispatch_aborted`, as its thrown timing does today (spec 009). `on_inbound_frame` returns `store_cancelled`, through §9's catch, and so do `Session::open` and `Session::drive_reconnect` for the initiator's Logon emission (item 9). Each path has one code on both timings.
4. **Any store.** The ending applies to a store that is not persistent too. Today such a store's returned `store_cancelled` is transmitted over, as every failed store call on a volatile store is.
5. **A fact for sign-off: v0.9 clears a cancellation recorded on the calling thread at three places.** In each, the first statement after the handler is `reset_cancellation_state()`, which installs what a cancelled lock wait leaves. (a) After `on_inbound_active_`'s catch, for every exception that catch takes (D-CAN-3). (b) After `Session::send`'s catch, for every exception that catch takes, not only the store's: a cancellation met by a pre-check at `send_impl`, at `Session::close` when `toApp` threw, at the sequence-number lock, at the store call (D-OUT-2), or at `store_then_emit`'s await of the transmit step (route (w0)); and any exception that is not a cancellation (D-OUT-9). (c) After the handler in `emit_initiator_logon_`, for every exception that handler takes, at any await of the function (D-OUT-5, as R-20 widens it). If B45 (fixpp#583) changes what a lock leaves on its caller's thread, all three must be removed or re-aligned with it; the pair cells of §5 report a change on one side alone.
6. **A session with neither a live transport nor a callback sink keeps today's logout wait (the round's C-2).** `store_then_emit`'s transmit step runs no statement for such a session and returns success, so every frame is reported as sent, the graceful Logout among them, and the phase records `LogoutSent` and waits out its timeout (D-OUT-6). It is read here as outside R-17's routes: the session has no transport that a frame could be handed to. R-17's Reading, "every route on which the phase's Logout was not handed to the transport", can be read to cover it; if the owner reads it so, the change is one test ahead of the wait and one rewritten cell. Such a session is a `Session` driven directly with no `transport_send` configured; the cell that depends on the wait is `FsmTransitionMatrixTest.LogoutSent_InboundNonLogoutEventsAreDrainedCellRemainsLogoutSent`.
7. **An inbound arm that is already running when the session ends may still send its remaining replies (Probe 8).** It exists today and is not specific to a cancelled store: no reply leaf reads the session's state before it sends, and `Disconnected` does not close the transport (fixpp#534). This section changes only which of those replies go out and with which numbers: one assigned before the cancellation is not transmitted, on any store; one assigned after it is numbered from the cancelled frame's number and is transmitted, when no other frame's reconcile is in flight (D-OUT-10, "What the compare does not test"). So what the peer reads from such an arm still depends on where the arm stood when the session ended, as it does today. **With another frame's reconcile in flight it also depends on an order at the store's lock (R-23; measured, Probe 9a).** On a `FileStore`, with a second send queued behind the cancelled one (or behind one whose write failed; D-OUT-10): when that send reached the store's lock ahead of the arm's inbound persist, the peer reads neither reply, and the arm's first reply stays in the store unsent; when the persist reached it first, the peer reads both replies. No number is on the wire twice and none is missing from what was sent. R-23 settles it with fixpp#534's class: this batch states it and pins it with one cell, and that issue's batch removes it. Making either dependence one ending needs a test of the session's state on the send path, which is fixpp#534's subject. Not changed here.
8. **R-19's two statements are measured in the probe's form, not in this note's.** Probe 8 built the take-back and the compare with the returns today's code has. It did not build D-OUT-2's exception, the handlers of D-OUT-5 and D-OUT-6, or §9's catch. The note decides the two statements together with those; that form is not built, and the zero-allocation gates and the full suite run on it at implementation (§5, "Implementation steps that can send the design back").
9. **The initiator's Logon emission returns `store_cancelled` for a cancellation, where v0.9 said `dispatch_aborted` (R-20).** R-20 gives the function one ending and names no code. Its hydration read and its `reset_on_logon` reset return the store's own code on the returned timing today, so `store_cancelled` is the one code that is the same on both timings at every await of the function (D-OUT-5). It changes what a cancellation thrown at the Logon's store returns today (`dispatch_aborted`). The triage's cell for this root asserts `dispatch_aborted`; this note asserts `store_cancelled`, with the reason under `## Gate A`.
10. **A frame that D-OUT-10's compare stops returns `dispatch_aborted`; on a persistent store the same frame keeps 059's ending and code.** Measured (Probe 8) for a second send in flight: `dispatch_aborted` on a `MemoryStore`, `store_seqnum_out_of_order` on a `FileStore`. The wire is the same on both: the frame is not transmitted.
11. **A frame in its store call when a close's teardown reset runs is no longer handed to a callback transport's sink** (D-OUT-10, the table of writers). On a live transport nothing changes: `close` has closed the transport before the reset. It holds when nothing assigns a number between the reset and the frame's return (D-OUT-10's table). Reasoned, not run; §5 has its cell, "D-OUT-10, a frame across a close's teardown reset, on the callback transport", with its mutant.
12. **Ruled by R-22: a cancellation that reaches `Session::drive_reconnect` before the Logon emission is fixed in its own batch** (fixpp#585, B47, after B28 merges). R-20 covers `emit_initiator_logon_`. `drive_reconnect` awaits its connect attempt first; a cancellation that reaches it there ends as a returned error or as a thrown exception, by timing. This section changes nothing ahead of the Logon emission and states the boundary with its routes (§10.5).
13. **The take-back and the compare are exact under a requirement on stores (R-21).** They are exact when a `store()` call that is not ended by a cancellation returns only after every `store()` call the session made earlier for that direction has returned. A call that a cancellation ended may return before earlier calls: the `FileStore` returns a call cancelled at its lock while an earlier call is still held in its write, which every cell of §10 that cancels a queued call shows. That is how R-21's words "return in call order" are read (§0): without the exception they are false of the `FileStore` today, and the option also says "no code beyond v0.10". Both shipped stores keep the sentence in that form. A store that returns its calls out of order can put one number on the wire twice, and such a store already sends frames out of order today. By R-21 the requirement is one sentence in `message_store.hpp` (D-OUT-7), a row for store implementers and a control on the `FileStore`; no guard is added in the session, and no code beyond v0.10.

### 10.1 What happens today

- **Read (T0A.12; re-read for this section).** `store_then_emit` handles the results of its store call in different ways. A returned `store_cancelled`, from any store, is absorbed: the frame is transmitted. Any other returned error fails closed on a persistent store (059): the number is reconciled from the store, the error is returned, nothing is transmitted. A thrown `operation_aborted` returns `dispatch_aborted` from any store, with nothing transmitted and the number left consumed.
- **Measured (T0A.13; Probe 7), an application's own cancellation of `Engine::send` on a `FileStore` session:**

| Observation | Returned (cancelled at the store's lock) | Thrown (cancelled at the store call's first suspension) |
|---|---|---|
| The frame, number n | transmitted, and read by the peer ahead of the held frame n − 1 | not transmitted |
| The store | no frame for n; its next outbound number is n | the same |
| `Engine::send` | success | `dispatch_aborted` |
| The session | `Active` | `Disconnected` |
| The next outbound number in memory | n + 1 | n + 1 |
| A ResendRequest for n | one GapFill over it | not asked: the session is not `Active` |
| The next `Engine::send` | refused by the store as out of order; `Disconnected`; the number reconciled down to n | refused: the session is not `Active` |
| A fresh session on the same store | starts at n, a number the peer has already read on an application message | starts at n, which the peer has not read |

- **Who can reach it, read (T0A.12).** The cancellation is the one emitted on the thread that awaits the store call: an application's own cancellation of its `Engine::send`; `Engine::stop`'s emit, for a reply the role loop's thread is storing; `Session::close`'s emit, for a liveness frame; the grace timer or an escalating terminal close, for a graceful close's Logout; a direct driver's own.
  - The returned timing needs the store call to queue at the store's lock, which needs a store that holds its lock across a suspension and a second store call of the same session in flight: a `FileStore`, or a third-party store. During the wait the lock sequence accepts every type of cancellation.
  - The thrown timing needs the cancellation to be recorded before the store's lock is reached. Both shipped stores suspend once before their lock (a leading post), and after `assign_outbound` the thread's filter is terminal; so a terminal cancellation can land there, and `Engine::stop`'s total one cannot.
- **Feature 059 kept the absorb on a premise that does not hold.** Its D7 calls the returned code "shutdown-only". An application's cancellation of a send is not a shutdown, and it reaches it (measured).
- **The graceful Logout, measured (Probe 4), on a `FileStore` session with one send held in its store:** when the Logout's store call is cancelled at the lock, by the grace timer or by an escalating terminal close, the Logout is transmitted ahead of the held frame and is not stored. The grace timer's one cancellation of the logout phase is spent at the lock. The phase then waits a full `logout_disconnect_timeout_ms` of its own on the session's clock, and `close(graceful)` and the escalating `close(terminal)` both wait for it.

### 10.2 Decisions

- **D-OUT-1 (R-15, R-16). One ending for a cancelled outbound store, on both timings and for any store.**

  | Observable | Both timings, after this section |
  |---|---|
  | The frame | not transmitted |
  | The store | holds no frame for the number; its next outbound number is unchanged |
