  | The next outbound number in memory, when the ending is taken | the cancelled frame's own, k, on both shipped stores, and on any store that keeps D-OUT-7's sentences, whatever later frame of the session is in flight (D-OUT-3, R-19, R-21). It is below k only when another writer lowered it first: the take-back of a lower cancelled frame, or a reset (D-OUT-10's table of writers) |
  | A frame of the session that was assigned a number above k and is in its own store call | not transmitted, on both shipped stores, and on any store that keeps D-OUT-7's sentences. On a store that is not persistent, by D-OUT-10's compare. On a persistent store the store refuses it as out of order and 059's route ends it without transmitting, as today |
  | The session | `Disconnected`, recorded in `store_then_emit` before it leaves; nothing is recorded when the caller is a superseded Logon arm (D-OUT-2) |
  | `onLogout` | fired by that write when the session was `Active`, once |
  | A later frame of the same call | none: the caller's frame is left by the exception |
  | Callbacks and events the arm orders after the reply | not run. Read for a peer's Logout in `Active`: `on_inbound_active_logout_` awaits its reply Logout's leaf first, and its sequence-reset event and the `fromAdmin` dispatch of the peer's Logout follow the leaf; when that reply's store is cancelled, neither runs, on either timing. Today both run on both timings (read by the round's triage, O-7). Recipe for the other arms: in each caller of D-OUT-4's table, the statements that follow its `store_then_emit` call |
  | A reply of an inbound arm of the session that was already running | not stopped by this section: D-OUT-10, "What the compare does not test" |
  | What the caller gets, and its thread after the call | per root of D-OUT-4's table, below. No root throws |

  | Root (D-OUT-4) | What its caller gets, both timings | The calling thread after the call, both timings |
  |---|---|---|
  | `Session::send`, and so `Engine::send` | `dispatch_aborted` | no cancellation recorded (D-OUT-9) |
  | `on_inbound_frame` | `store_cancelled`; success for a superseded Logon arm (D-CAN-4, D-CAN-5) | no cancellation recorded (D-CAN-3) |
  | `Session::open` and `Session::drive_reconnect`, for the initiator's Logon | `store_cancelled` (D-OUT-5; v0.9 said `dispatch_aborted`) | no cancellation recorded (D-OUT-5: the handler in `emit_initiator_logon_` is followed by the same reset) |
  | `Session::close(graceful)`, for the logout phase's Logout | success, as after a logout timeout (D-OUT-6) | not applicable: `close` disables cancellation on its own thread as its first statement, and the phase runs on a thread of `close`'s race, which ends with the phase |
  | `run_liveness_loop` | no caller: the loop is a detached coroutine, and it ends | not applicable: the thread ends with the loop |

  - **Why this ending.** It is the thrown timing's ending today in every row but the number, and R-15 and R-16 rule it. The returned timing's ending today puts a frame on the wire that the store does not hold, out of order, reports success, and leaves a session whose next send disconnects it and whose successor reuses a number the peer has read (measured, §10.1).
- **D-OUT-2 (R-15, R-16). In `store_then_emit`, both timings reach the same statements, and the function leaves by the exception.**
  - A returned `store_cancelled` is tested before the persistence gate, for any store. The handler for a thrown `operation_aborted` no longer returns. Each only notes that the store call was cancelled. After the `try`, one block runs for both: it takes the number back, by lowering the session's next outbound number to the frame's own (D-OUT-3); it calls `disconnect_unless_superseded_(arm)`; it throws `asio::system_error` with `operation_aborted`, the exception the thrown timing carried.
  - **Nothing is transmitted** because the block precedes the transmit step and does not fall through to it.
  - **Why an exception and not a returned code.** A returned code reaches the session's state only through what each caller does with it, and several callers discard it (D-OUT-4's table marks each). On the returned timing such a caller would go on: a later reply of the same arm could be transmitted, and the frame's inbound persist would run. On the thrown timing it stops at its next await, because the thread is still cancelled. An exception leaves every caller's frame the same way on both timings, whatever the caller does with results. That is what "by construction" means here: no caller can be written that continues past a cancelled store.
  - **Why `store_then_emit` records the state itself.** With an exception no caller's error branch runs, and one root's handler returns without a state write (the liveness loop's, D-OUT-5). Recording at the one site gives `Disconnected` at every root. 059's D3 made the transition caller-owned so that a returned error had the shape of a failed write's; this route no longer returns.
  - **The superseded Logon arm keeps today's result exactly.** For a caller that passed `arm` and is superseded, today's returned timing does not transmit, records nothing and returns success. After this section: not transmitted, nothing recorded (`disconnect_unless_superseded_`), and `on_inbound_frame` returns success (D-CAN-5). The number is taken back, which today it is not. One case is excepted from "today's result exactly": a reply Logon that is in its store call across a close's teardown reset is stopped by D-OUT-10's compare, which sits ahead of the superseded test, and gets `dispatch_aborted` where today it gets success (D-OUT-10, "Where it sits").
  - **No reset here.** `store_then_emit` does not touch the thread's cancellation state: a reset inside it would let a caller that discards results go on past the cancelled store. The reset belongs to the entry point that ends the call: D-CAN-3 on the inbound path, D-OUT-9 in `Session::send`.
  - **Against the constitution** (`.specify/constitution.md`, read for this decision). No article forbids an exception inside the library or requires `noexcept`: Article XI names coroutines and "ASIO native cancellation slots end-to-end", and Article XV's banned patterns list none of the two. Its entry on message loss, "silent loss desynchronises the sequence-number contract", is what this decision serves: the frame is not sent, the caller is told, the number is not consumed. Appendix A lists "Error semantics | new `fixpp_error_t` value, exception-vs-`expected` change" among the triggers of the four mandatory controls; this is such a change, on a private function, and §7 says where each control stands. No waiver is needed. The rule the code states for itself is in `Session::close`'s closing comment: cancellation surfaces as an error value "never a thrown exception across parse→fromApp (I-09)". That holds at every public entry for this exception: it is caught at each root of D-OUT-4 and none of `Session::send`, `on_inbound_frame`, `open`, `drive_reconnect` or `close` throws it. §10.5 gives each public entry its row, with what can still leave one by an exception and whose that is.
  - **The declaration drops `noexcept`**, as `send_impl` has none: the body now ends one route by an exception. `noexcept` on a coroutine covers the call that creates it and not its body (D-536-3), so no caller's behaviour changes with the keyword. The function is a private member declared in the installed `session.hpp`.
  - **Unchanged:** a store call that succeeds; 059's fail-closed route for every other returned error on a persistent store, with its reconcile; the proceed for every other returned error on a volatile store, which then meets D-OUT-10's compare ahead of the transmit step; a store body's exception that is not a cancellation, which is absorbed and transmitted over as today. A cancellation that lands on 059's reconcile read is that route's, not this one: the store call had failed, and the ending is 059's, with the number possibly left one ahead (`B-059-1`, "Residual window").
- **D-OUT-3 (R-16, R-19). The number is taken back, in memory, without an await, whatever other frame of the session is in flight.**
  - **Why not 059's reconcile.** It reads the store and writes the counter, and awaits both. On the thrown timing the thread is cancelled, so neither can be awaited in `store_then_emit` without resetting the state there, which D-OUT-2 excludes. One mechanism for both timings therefore cannot await.
  - **The mechanism (R-19).** `SeqnumManager` gains one member, `take_back_outbound(k)`, that is not a coroutine and takes no lock. Given the cancelled frame's number k, it lowers the next outbound number to k when that number is above k. It never raises it.
  - **Why it lowers, and is not a plain assignment.** Two frames of one session can be in their store calls together and both be cancelled (D-OUT-10 says how they come to overlap). When the lower one, j, is handled first, the next number is j; an assignment at the handling of k would then raise it to k and leave j consumed. Lowering gives j in either order. Read from Probe 8's candidate, which built the member in this form and states this reason; the order "the lower one first" was not driven.
  - **v0.9's form is withdrawn (R-19).** v0.9 took the number back only when no later number had been assigned, and left the counter alone when one had. On a store that is not persistent the later frame was then transmitted after the session had ended, the cancelled frame never was, and the number stayed consumed. That is R-16's premise unmet, and it is today's wire (measured at the base, Probe 8, the two `MemoryStore` cells). What makes the unconditioned form safe is D-OUT-10: the later frame is not transmitted, so the number that is taken back is not handed out for a second frame on the wire.
  - **What a persistent store gives, with or without D-OUT-10.** The later frame's store call is refused as out of order, because the store's next number is still k. 059's route then reconciles the counter to the store's next number, which is k, and returns without transmitting (read by the round's triage, C-1; measured, Probe 8, the `FileStore` cell with a second send). The peer sees neither a hole nor a number twice.
  - **Why no lock is needed.** It runs on the session's serialisation domain, where no critical section of `SeqnumManager` suspends (§9.2), so no coroutine holds the mutex while another runs. `peek_outbound` reads the same field under the same condition and says so in its comment.
  - **The premise: this store call stored nothing.** Per route, read:
    - the thrown timing at the pre-check in `store_then_emit`'s own frame: the store's body never starts;
    - the thrown timing from inside `FileStore::store` or `MemoryStore::store`: each can let `operation_aborted` out only before it holds its lock (the pre-check of `FileStore`'s lock, after its leading post; part 1 of the lock macro in `MemoryStore`). `FileStore::store` catches an `operation_aborted` from its offload and reports the write as done; it has no await after it;
    - the returned timing: each shipped store returns `store_cancelled` only from a failed lock, before its critical section;
    - measured for a `FileStore` on both timings (T0A.13): `retrieve` reports a gap for the number, and the store's next outbound number is the number.
  - **A third-party store** is bound by D-OUT-7's sentence. One that returns `store_cancelled`, or lets `operation_aborted` out of its body, after it has stored breaks the premise: the take-back then lowers the session's number below the store's. D-OUT-7 states what follows. Not measured (Probe 8, "Not measured").
  - **What is sent next.**
    - A fresh `Session` on the same persistent store hydrates its outbound number from the store: k, a number the peer has not read. Today, on the returned timing, the peer has read it (measured, Probe 7).
    - A `Session` reconnected in place by its own driver, on a store that is not persistent, sends the number its counter holds: k, or the number after the last reply that an arm already running sent (D-OUT-10). Reasoned; no fixture reconnects a `Session` in place (Probe 7, "Not measured").
    - The held frame k − 1, whose store call was in flight: its store completes and it is transmitted if the transport is still open, after the session was recorded `Disconnected`. A frame stored and sent, in order; its number is below the next one, so D-OUT-10's compare passes it. Reasoned from `store_then_emit`, which tests no state before its transmit step.
- **D-OUT-10 (R-19). A frame whose number is no longer valid when its own store call has returned is not transmitted.**
  - **The statement.** In `store_then_emit`, on the route that goes on to the transmit step after a store call was made: when the frame's number is not below the session's next outbound number (`peek_outbound()`), the frame is not transmitted and `dispatch_aborted` is returned. It is one compare of two integers, with no await.
  - **Where it sits, and why there.** After 059's fail-closed return, so that a frame a persistent store refused keeps 059's reconcile and 059's code. Inside the branch that made a store call, so that a frame sent with no store call does not run it: a session with no store, and the frame whose store call `store_then_emit` skips. That is the probe's placement.
  - **It sits ahead of `store_then_emit`'s superseded test, so one superseded Logon arm's result changes (the round's O-2).** That test follows the store block (read). A reply Logon that is in its store call across a close's teardown reset is superseded and has a number at or above 1; the compare returns `dispatch_aborted` ahead of the test, where the test returns success today, and D-CAN-5 converts `store_cancelled` only. A direct driver sees the code; under an `Engine` the session ends the same way. The compare is not moved: Probe 8 built this placement.
  - **Why the condition identifies the frame, and the two conditions it needs.** `assign_outbound` hands out the next number and raises it, and every production caller of `store_then_emit` passes a number it assigned in the same function, ahead of the call (read, Probe 8). So a frame in flight has a number below the next one for as long as no writer lowers the counter. The table of writers below says which writers can lower it while a frame is in its store call. After a writer has lowered it, a frame that was assigned a number at or above the new one and is still in its store call is *stale*, and the compare stops it only if no later `assign_outbound` has raised the counter past it by the time its store call returns. That needs two conditions.
    - *The store's calls return in call order:* D-OUT-7's order sentence (R-21).
    - *After a take-back only one coroutine still assigns: an inbound arm that was already running.* Read by the round's triage (review index 7, C-1): `Session::send` tests the state first; `run_liveness_loop` tests the state after each sleep and again after its Heartbeat's emit; and at each `assign_outbound()` site of `session.cpp` no suspension separates the `peek_outbound()` that stamps the frame from the assign: either no await lies between the two, or the one await between them is on a branch that returns before the assign. Recipe: `grep -n 'assign_outbound()' src/session/session.cpp`, each site read back to its peek.
    - *The argument, for the shipped stores.* Reasoned from those readings (the round's triage, C-1); not run. A stale frame's store call was made before the take-back, so on a `MemoryStore` its one post is queued ahead of anything a frame assigned later posts. The arm assigns its second number only after its first reply's store call has returned, which is after the stale frame's, or that store call was ended by a cancellation, and then D-OUT-2's exception ends the arm and no second number is assigned. So while a stale frame is in flight the counter is at most one above the number it was lowered to, and the compare stops the frame. On a `FileStore` the stale frame is refused under the store's lock, which grants in arrival order (taken from the lock's comments, and pinned by the control of §5), and 059's return precedes the compare. A session's strand keeps this order with more than one I/O thread; an executor that runs one session on two threads at once is outside every table of this note.
  - **How two frames come to overlap, read (the round's triage, C-1).** `send_impl` and every reply leaf assign a number and only then await `store_then_emit`; nothing is held across the two. Both shipped stores suspend before their lock. A second `Session::send` that finds the frame slot held takes `send_fallback_leaf_`, which exists for it. So frame k + 1 can be assigned and enter its own store call while frame k is suspended in its own.
  - **A value, not D-OUT-2's exception.** The frame's own store call was not cancelled, so its condition is not D-OUT-1's; the session's ending was taken by the frame that was cancelled. `Session::send` returns the value as it returns any failed emit; a reply leaf does with it what it does with any failed emit, and one that discards it goes on to its next statement. Built and measured in this form (Probe 8). A frame the compare stops writes a second `Disconnected` entry to the state ring: `Session::send` records the state for a returned `dispatch_aborted` with no test, and `run_liveness_loop` does so for a failed emit, and the stale frame's sender reaches those writes after frame k's block has recorded the state (read by the round's triage, O-3). D-OUT-9's test is on the exception's route only.
  - **Every writer of the session's next outbound number, and whether a frame can be in its store call when it writes.** Recipe: `grep -n 'next_outbound_' include/fixpp/session/seqnum_manager.hpp src/session/seqnum_manager.cpp` gives the members that write the field; for each, `grep -n 'seqnum_mgr_\.<member>' src/session/session.cpp` gives its callers. Read by Probe 8 and carried here; re-derive before relying on it.

    | Writer | From | A frame in its store call when it writes? | What the compare then does |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `assign_outbound` | every sender | yes. It raises the number; it cannot raise it past a stale frame while that frame is in flight, under D-OUT-7's order sentence | nothing for a frame that no lowering writer has passed: it stays below the number. A stale frame is stopped, under the two conditions above |
    | `hydrate` | `ensure_hydrated_`, once per session, before any number is assigned; again at each Logon emission when `refresh_on_logon` is set, which only a `Session` reconnected in place reaches (fixpp#578's class) | no | — |
    | `reset_to_one`, for `reset_on_logon` | `reset_seqnums_to_one_durable`, on the Logon paths | no under an `Engine`: the session is not `Active` and has sent nothing on the connection | — |
    | `reset_to_one` and `set_next_outbound`, in a reset unit | `run_reset_unit_` | no under an `Engine` | — |
    | `set_next_outbound`, in 059's reconcile | `store_then_emit`'s fail-closed route | yes. It writes the store's next number d, read under the store's lock and applied after the read returns. A frame below d is stored and passes the compare. A frame above d is refused by the store. A frame numbered d that was assigned after the read and before the write is stored and then stopped by the compare: stored, not transmitted | that one frame's sender gets `dispatch_aborted`, and the frame stays in the store unsent. Measured (Probe 9a); the trace is under the table |
    | `reset_to_one`, in the teardown reset | `Session::close`, its terminal part | **yes** | the frame is not transmitted, when nothing assigns a number between the reset and the frame's return. An arm that is still running and assigns after the reset and before `close` drains the manager lets a frame numbered 1 in flight pass the compare; callback transport and a direct driver only (reasoned; the round's O-4). On a live transport `close` has closed the transport before the reset, so the frame's write fails with or without the compare. On the callback transport today's code hands the frame to the sink after the close; after this decision it does not |
    | `take_back_outbound` | D-OUT-2's block | yes, by intent | the later frame is not transmitted |
    | `set_counters` | tests only | — | — |

  - **059's reconcile can write the counter over a number that was assigned after the take-back (R-23; measured, Probe 9a).** The verify record, "Probe 9a"; evidence under the parent's `research/probes/b28_rulings/p9a/`.
    - **The scene.** Probe 8's `FileStore` scene with a second send. Frame h is an `Engine::send` held inside its `store()`'s offload, so the store's lock stays taken. Queued behind it: frame k, an `Engine::send` bound to a cancellation signal of the cell's; frame k + 1, a second `Engine::send`; and the inbound persist of an arm that answers with a Reject and then a Logout, through leaves that discard the emit's result. Frame k is then cancelled at the lock, and the hold is released.
    - **Order 1, frame k + 1 queued ahead of the arm's inbound persist, with the probe's candidate.** The take-back sets the next number to k. Frame k + 1's `store()` is granted the lock and refused, and its thread starts the reconcile's read, which queues behind the persist. The persist returns; the arm's Reject is assigned k, and the next number is k + 1. The reconcile's read returns k, and its write lowers the next number from k + 1 to k. The Reject's `store()` is accepted. The compare reads k against k and does not transmit it. The Logout is assigned k again; the store, which now expects k + 1, refuses it, and 059's route ends it. **The peer reads neither reply, and the store holds a Reject at k that was never transmitted.**
    - **Order 2, the persist queued ahead of frame k + 1, with the candidate.** The Reject's `store()` reaches the lock ahead of the reconcile's read, is stored, and is transmitted with the next number at k + 1. The reconcile then reads k + 1 and changes nothing, and the Logout carries k + 1. The peer reads the Reject at k and the Logout at k + 1. This is what Probe 8's cell measured with no second send, one number up.
    - **In both orders, with the candidate:** frame k's sender gets `dispatch_aborted`; frame k + 1's sender gets `store_seqnum_out_of_order`; the session is `Disconnected`; `onLogout` fired once; the peer then reads end-of-stream.
    - **On the unedited tree the two orders do not differ.** Frame k's returned `store_cancelled` is absorbed, so frame k is transmitted ahead of the held frame and its sender is told success. The Reject is refused by the store. The reconcile lowers the next number to k, and the Logout goes out numbered k. The peer reads one MsgSeqNum twice on one connection, on an application message and then on the Logout.
    - **The control:** the same two orders with frame k released in place of cancelled. Every sender is told success, the peer reads every frame in order, and no reconcile runs.
    - **The probe's limits, as its record states them.** The order of arrival at the `FileStore`'s lock is taken from the order in which the wrapper's body was entered and an exhausted poll between two starts; the arrival itself is not observed. The order of the grants is read from the order of the returns. A debug build, one thread, the plain transport. Not measured: what a fresh session's peer gets when it asks for k after Order 1 (the round's triage reasons a GapFill over the stored Reject); the candidate together with §10's exception; a `MemoryStore`, on which 059's reconcile does not run. No fix was built.
    - **What is new, and what is not.** The reconcile is 059's, and its read can be stale today (`B-059-1`, "Residual window"). New is that the store accepts the frame, because the take-back had aligned the two numbers, and that the compare then stops it. A first reconcile aligns the two numbers as the take-back does. So the same ending is reached with no cancelled store: a frame whose write failed once, a second frame refused behind it, and an arm whose inbound persist reached the store's lock between their two reconcile reads. At the base the arm's first reply is transmitted there; after this batch it is stored and not sent. Reasoned from `store_then_emit` and `FileStore::store`; not run. It is the same branch, the same consequence and the same class (R-23). No number is on the wire twice, and none is missing from what was sent.
    - **Where it is settled (R-23).** It is part of what an inbound arm already running may still send after the session has ended. The split disappears when a `Disconnected` session stops sending, which is fixpp#534's subject. This batch states it and pins it with one cell (§5); that issue's batch removes it.
  - **The teardown reset's row is decided, not left: the compare is not narrowed for it.** A frame that was in its store call across a close's teardown reset carries a number of the sequence that the reset ended. Handing it to a sink after the session was closed and its numbers reset is not an ending this note wants to keep, and the session it belonged to has ended. A direct driver that sends while it closes gets `dispatch_aborted` for that send. Reasoned, not run; §5 has its cell, "D-OUT-10, a frame across a close's teardown reset, on the callback transport", and its mutant, the compare skipped for a closing session. No cell at the base holds a frame in its store call across that reset on the callback transport, and no cell of the full suite failed because of the compare (measured, Probe 8). §10's block lists it for the owner and the B&L row states it.
  - **A `Session` driven directly can be handed an inbound frame while its own Logon is in its store call**, and a reset unit of that frame could then lower the number under the Logon, which the compare would stop. Tests only: under an `Engine` the Logon emission has returned before the pump delivers a frame. Read (Probe 8); no cell fails for it. **Waived, with this reason:** it needs an inbound Logon delivered before the session's own Logon has left its store call, which no caller under an `Engine` delivers: the read pump starts after the Logon emission has returned. Only a direct driver that hands a frame to `on_inbound_frame` during `open` reaches it.
  - **What the compare does not test: the session's state. An inbound arm that is already running goes on.**
    - **Today, read.** After `Disconnected` no new call assigns a number: `Session::send` returns `session_invalid_state_for_send`, the liveness loop tests the state after each sleep, and a `Disconnected` session drains new inbound frames (read by the round's triage, C-1). An arm that is past its own state read when another coroutine of the session records `Disconnected` is not stopped: no reply leaf, and no statement of `store_then_emit`, reads the state before a frame is assigned, stored or transmitted, and `Disconnected` does not close the transport (fixpp#534). Its remaining replies go out while the transport is open. Measured at the base (Probe 8, both arm cells on the unedited tree): the peer read the arm's Logout after frame k's send had recorded `Disconnected`, on both stores.
    - **So it is general, and it exists today.** A failed `Session::send` and the liveness loop also record `Disconnected` while an arm can be suspended; nothing in it is specific to a cancelled store. D-OUT-2's exception does not reach the arm: it unwinds the coroutine whose store call was cancelled, and the arm is another coroutine. R-19 rules on the number, not on this. The boundary of this batch: a frame that is already assigned or already running when the session ends is not recalled by B28, beyond the one test of D-OUT-10. It is the class of fixpp#534: once `Disconnected` closes the transport, such a reply meets a closed transport. Reasoned.
    - **What this section changes inside it.** Which of the arm's replies go out, and with which numbers. A reply that was assigned before the cancellation carries a number above k and is not transmitted, on any store. A reply that is assigned after it is numbered from k and is transmitted, when no other frame's reconcile is in flight. Neither statement tests the session's state (measured, Probe 8, the candidate's two arm cells).
    - **The same wire on both stores, for the same suspension point, when no other frame's reconcile is in flight: reasoned, and measured at one point per store.** With another frame's reconcile in flight, a `FileStore`'s wire depends on which reached the store's lock first, that frame or the arm's inbound persist: Order 1 and Order 2 above (measured, Probe 9a).
      - *The arm is suspended in its first reply's store call, and that reply holds the number after k.* Measured on a `MemoryStore`: the reply is not transmitted, and the arm's next reply, its Logout, carries k. On a `FileStore`, reasoned from 059's route as read above: the store refuses the reply, the counter is reconciled to k, nothing is transmitted, and the next reply carries k. The same wire. What the first reply's leaf gets differs by store: `dispatch_aborted` from the compare, the store's own code from 059's route (measured for a second send on each store). A leaf that returns its emit's result passes that difference to the caller of `on_inbound_frame`.
      - *The arm is suspended at its inbound persist, and no reply has a number yet.* Measured on a `FileStore`: the arm's Reject carries k and its Logout the number after it; both are stored and both are transmitted. On a store that is not persistent this point does not exist: `persist_inbound_advance_` returns before its store call there (read).
      - The probe that would measure the first point on a `FileStore` is Probe 8's arm cell with the arm held at its first reply's store call. Whether a gate can hold it there while frame k is still in its own store call is not established: that arm's inbound persist comes first and takes the same lock. Not run.
    - Not taken: a test of the session's state in `store_then_emit`, ahead of the transmit step. It would change what every sender does after `Disconnected`, which is fixpp#534's subject; R-23 leaves it there, and it was not probed.
  - **What Probe 8 built, what it compared, and what it returned** (the verify record, "Probe 8"; evidence under the parent's `research/probes/b28_rulings/p8/`).
    - **Built:** `take_back_outbound` as D-OUT-3 states it, called at both timings of a cancelled store in `store_then_emit` (a returned `store_cancelled`, tested ahead of the persistence gate; the handler for a thrown `operation_aborted`), and the compare, placed as above. Each of those sites returns `dispatch_aborted`. Every other return is today's.
    - **Not built:** D-OUT-2's exception and its state write, the handlers of D-OUT-5 and D-OUT-6, D-OUT-9's reset, and §9's catch.
    - **Compared:** the unedited tree against the candidate. One scene cell for each pair of store kind and second frame. The interceptor registrations of fixpp#544 on `linux-gcc-release` and `linux-clang-release`, with a positive control. The full debug `ctest`, by name, against a set of failing tests written down before the run.
    - **The scene.** An acceptor under an `Engine` on the plain transport; the session's next outbound number is k. The gate uses no sleep and no store hook: from inside frame k's `toApp` the cell posts two handlers to the session's executor. The first starts the second frame: another `Engine::send`, or a Heartbeat with a SendingTime(52) outside the threshold handed to `on_inbound_frame`, whose arm answers with a Reject and then a Logout through leaves that discard the emit's result. The second emits a terminal cancellation on frame k's signal. Two `Engine::send` calls spawned from outside the session do not overlap on one I/O thread (seen with an earlier form of the probe; that output was not kept).

    | Store, and the second frame | Unedited tree | Candidate |
    |---|---|---|
    | `MemoryStore`, a second `Engine::send` | the second send succeeds; the peer reads its frame, numbered one above k, and never a frame numbered k; the next number in memory is two above k | the second send returns `dispatch_aborted`; the peer reads neither frame; the next number is k |
    | `MemoryStore`, an inbound arm | the peer reads the Reject and the Logout, numbered one and two above k, and never a frame numbered k; the next number is the one after the Logout's | the peer reads one frame, the Logout, numbered k; the next number is the one after it |
    | `FileStore`, a second `Engine::send` | the second send returns `store_seqnum_out_of_order`; the peer reads nothing; the next number in memory and in the store is k | the same |
    | `FileStore`, an inbound arm (suspended at its inbound persist) | the peer reads one frame, the Logout, numbered k; the store holds it | the peer reads the Reject, numbered k, and then the Logout; the store holds both |

    - **In every cell, on both trees:** frame k's send returns `dispatch_aborted`; the session is `Disconnected`; `onLogout` fired once; `on_inbound_frame` returned success; the peer then read end-of-stream.
    - **The gates.** With the candidate, the registrations of W-A, W-B, W-E and W-D-W's real-chain twin pass on both presets. The positive control, a large local kept across the store's await, fails W-E, the real-chain twin and the veto registration, so these gates see `store_then_emit`'s frame. The frame's size is the unedited tree's on both compilers.
    - **The suite.** The set of failing tests equals the set predicted, by name, in both directions. It holds the binaries that are RED by design at this point of the batch, the existing cells that pin a cancelled store's present ending (§10.4), and a check that fails for a reason outside the probe. No failure is caused by the compare.
    - **Not measured:** an executor that runs one session on more than one thread; the exception of D-OUT-2 together with the two statements; the callback transport across a close's teardown reset; a third-party store that does not keep D-OUT-7's sentence; TLS; ASan and TSan; MSVC; a full suite on a release tree.
  - **What is measured and what is decided.** Measured, in the probe's form: the take-back, the compare, their placement, the scene cells, the gates and the suite. Decided by this note and not built: the same two statements with D-OUT-2's exception in place of the probe's returned `dispatch_aborted` at the cancelled frame, and with §9's catch above the inbound path. The gates that must run on that form are W-E and W-D-W's real-chain twin on both release presets and the full `ctest` compared by name; they are an implementation step with a return condition (§5).
  - Not taken (Codex's counter-proposals to the round's C-1): the reservation of a number serialised through the store call's completion, and a ledger of reserved numbers. The first puts a lock across the store call on the send path and removes the pipelining `send_fallback_leaf_` exists for; the second is new state for every send (the round's triage).
  - Not taken (Codex's counter-proposal in review index 7): an epoch, that is, a counter in `SeqnumManager` that every lowering write increments, read by a frame before its store call and compared after it. It closes one trace of D-OUT-7's, the refusal that returns late. It does not close the class: in the other trace, the later call that returns first, nothing has been lowered when the later frame is transmitted, so no epoch test fires. Alone it also stops a frame below k that is in flight (D-OUT-3's held frame k − 1, which this note transmits), and it cannot guard the take-back. It needs a local kept across the store's await, which Probe 8's candidate does not have, so every gate of the probe would run again. No test in `store_then_emit` restores order over a store that returns its calls out of order, short of the reservation serialised through the store call, which is declined above (the round's triage, C-1). R-21 settles the case as a requirement on stores.
- **D-OUT-4 (R-15, R-16). Every caller of `store_then_emit`, and what each does at a cancelled store.**
  - **How it was derived.** `git grep -n 'store_then_emit(' -- src include bindings`: every call is in `src/session/session.cpp`, and the function that holds each was named. There is no second store-then-transmit sequence: the walk transmits without storing; the reset unit, the durable reset and the inbound persist store without transmitting. `tests/support/session_test_access.hpp` holds a test seam that calls it.
  - **Roots.** Each call's chain of awaiting frames was followed to the first frame that holds a handler: its root. "Discards" means the call's result is cast to void today.

  | Caller | Frame | Result today | Root, and what it does with the exception |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `send_impl`, from `Session::send`, directly or through `send_fallback_leaf_` | application message | returned | `Session::send`'s catch: `Disconnected`, `dispatch_aborted`, as today, and D-OUT-9's reset |
  | `inbound_resend_request_leaf_`, `inbound_heartbeat_reply_leaf_`, `inbound_business_reject_leaf_`, both `emit_session_reject_` overloads, `inbound_acceptor_reply_logon_leaf_` | ResendRequest, Heartbeat, Business Message Reject, Reject, the reply Logon | returned, after a `Disconnected` write (the last two through `disconnect_unless_superseded_`) | D-CAN-1's catch |
  | `inbound_reject_leaf_`, `inbound_reject_best_effort_leaf_`, `apply_inbound_sequence_reset` | Reject | **discards** | D-CAN-1's catch |
  | `inbound_logout_leaf_`, `inbound_logon_sent_logout_leaf_`, `refuse_logon_with_logout_`, `honor_peer_next_expected_` (two sites) | Logout, among them D-537-5's | **discards** | D-CAN-1's catch |
  | `run_liveness_loop` (two sites) | Heartbeat, TestRequest | `Disconnected`, and the loop returns | the loop's own handler for a `std::system_error`, unchanged: the loop returns. The state was recorded by `store_then_emit` |
  | `emit_initiator_logon_`, from `Session::open` and `Session::drive_reconnect` | Logon | `Disconnected`, error returned | a new handler around the function's whole body (D-OUT-5, R-20) |
  | `run_logout_phase1` | the graceful Logout | **discards** | a new local handler (D-OUT-6) |

  - **Every root, and what happens to the exception there.** By the recipe above, the coroutines that can reach `store_then_emit` end at: `Session::send`; `on_inbound_active_`; `run_liveness_loop`; `emit_initiator_logon_`; `run_logout_phase1`; and the test seam, whose root is the test's own spawn. Each of the first five holds a handler in its own body (existing, or added by D-OUT-5 and D-OUT-6), so the exception reaches no `detached` completion: the role loops and the liveness loop are spawned `detached`, and a `detached` handler would drop it silently.
  - **Every `noexcept` function between the throw and its handler, as read from each definition.**
    - *Coroutines declared `noexcept`:* the reply leaves and the helpers that call `store_then_emit` on the inbound path (`emit_session_reject_`, `refuse_logon_with_logout_`, `apply_inbound_sequence_reset`, `honor_peer_next_expected_`), the sub-arms and the cold arms that await them, `dispose_unparseable_`, and the roots `Session::send`, `on_inbound_active_`, `run_liveness_loop`, `emit_initiator_logon_` and `run_logout_phase1`. On a coroutine the keyword covers the call that creates the frame; an exception that leaves the body is stored by asio's promise and rethrown in the awaiting frame (read in T0A.7 from asio's `awaitable.hpp`). So none of them terminates, and each passes the exception to its awaiter until a handler takes it.
    - *Coroutines not declared `noexcept`:* `send_impl`, `send_fallback_leaf_`, and `store_then_emit` after this decision.
    - *Plain functions declared `noexcept`:* `on_inbound_frame`, `on_inbound_cold_` and `on_inbound_active_slow_`. Each only returns the awaitable of the coroutine it selects and has returned before that coroutine's body runs, so the exception never passes through its body. Recipe: on each path, read the definition of every function named in D-OUT-4's table and of every function that awaits it, and class it by whether its body holds a `co_await` or `co_return`.
    - No plain `noexcept` function holds the `throw` or a `co_await` on any of these paths; the `throw` is in `store_then_emit`'s body. The static-analysis gate (`[const §IX.4]`) cannot speak of a `noexcept` coroutine whose body lets an exception through while the project's clang-tidy ruleset disables `bugprone-exception-escape` (read from `.clang-tidy`). The guard is review, and the cells of §5 whose exception crosses such a coroutine: "D-OUT-2, D-OUT-4, a caller that discards the result, both timings" and "D-OUT-2, a superseded Logon arm whose reply's store is cancelled".
  - **The proof that no caller is left out** is the recipe, not this table: every hit of the grep is in a function of one of the rows, and every row's root holds a handler. A new caller added later gets the exception whether or not it reads results; what it needs is a root with a handler, and the cell "D-OUT-4, every root" (§5) is the check.
- **D-OUT-5 (R-16, R-20). Each root that had no handler gets one; each root that had one keeps it.**
  - **`emit_initiator_logon_` holds one `try` around its whole body (R-20).** v0.9 put the handler at the function's `store_then_emit` call alone. The round's O-1 showed that the function's other awaits have the two endings R-14 removed on the inbound path, and R-20 rules that the handler covers the function. The handler takes every exception and only notes which kind it caught, as D-CAN-2's does. After it, in this order: D-OUT-9's reset; `Disconnected`, when the state is not already `Disconnected`; the return of `store_cancelled` for a cancellation and of `dispatch_aborted` for any other exception. Without the handler the exception would leave `Session::open` or `Session::drive_reconnect`. Without the reset the two timings would leave the caller's thread in different states, as D-OUT-9 says of `Session::send`.
  - **Why `store_cancelled`, where v0.9's handler returned `dispatch_aborted`.** It is D-CAN-4's derivation, made on this function. R-20 gives the function one ending and names no code. Its hydration reads, through `ensure_hydrated_`, and the store's `reset()`, through the fatal reset, have a returned timing that passes the store's own code to the caller today. One handler cannot tell which await threw. So the one code that agrees with the returned timing at every await is `store_cancelled`; with `dispatch_aborted` at the handler, a cancelled hydration read would return one code or the other by timing. What it changes against today: a cancellation thrown at the Logon's store returns `store_cancelled` where it returns `dispatch_aborted`. An exception that is not a cancellation returns `dispatch_aborted`, as at D-CAN-2.
  - **No supersession on this path.** The function passes no arm to `ensure_hydrated_` or to `store_then_emit`, so each of their state writes is unconditional, as the function's own are (read). The handler's write needs no predicate of D-CAN-5's kind.
  - **Every await of the function, and both timings after this decision.** Recipe: every `co_await` in the body of `emit_initiator_logon_`, in order; re-derive it the same way.

    | Await | Returned timing: is there one, and what the function does with it (today, kept) | Thrown timing today | Both timings after |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | The hydration reads (`ensure_hydrated_`, on a persistent store) | yes, when the read queues at the store's lock. `ensure_hydrated_` clears `hydrating_`, records `Disconnected` and returns the store's code; the function returns it | `operation_aborted` leaves `open` or `drive_reconnect`; the state stays what it was (`LogonSent` under `open`); the thread stays cancelled; `hydrating_` stays set, so a later hydration on the same `Session` skips the store | `Disconnected`; `store_cancelled`; `hydrating_` clear (D-CAN-1's guard in `ensure_hydrated_`); nothing thrown; no cancellation recorded on the calling thread |
    | The reset of `reset_on_logon` (`reset_seqnums_to_one_durable(fatal)`) | yes, at the store's `reset()`, when it queues at the store's lock: the function records `Disconnected` and returns the store's code. None at the sequence-number lock (§9.2) | as above, with no flag involved | `Disconnected`; `store_cancelled`; nothing thrown; the thread clear |
    | The sequence-number locks: `assign_outbound`, `hydrate` inside `ensure_hydrated_`, and `reset_to_one` inside the fatal reset | none (§9.2) | as above | the same. The name is wider than the event, as D-CAN-4 says of that kind |
    | The Logon's store (`store_then_emit`) | yes. Today the code is absorbed and the Logon is transmitted | `store_then_emit` returns `dispatch_aborted`; the function records `Disconnected` and returns it; the thread stays cancelled | D-OUT-2's exception on both, taken by the handler: the Logon is not transmitted, the number is taken back, `Disconnected` (recorded in `store_then_emit`), `store_cancelled`, the thread clear |
    | The Logon's transmit step | the routes of D-CAN-6's case 2 | route (w0), the pre-check of `store_then_emit`'s await of the write, throws out of `open` or `drive_reconnect` | route (w0) now ends at the handler: `Disconnected`, `store_cancelled`. Routes (w1) to (w3) return `dispatch_aborted` from `store_then_emit`; the function records `Disconnected` and returns it, with the thread as the route left it. Not one ending: it is fixpp#584's, by ruling R-18 |

    - The returned timings are read from the function and from `ensure_hydrated_`. That each needs a second store call of the session in flight, or a third-party store's own wait, is the condition §10.1 gives for the outbound store; under an `Engine` a session that has not sent its Logon has none in flight (reasoned). The thrown timing at the first hydration read is reachable under an `Engine`, reasoned by the round's triage (O-1): the connect and the handshake leave the thread accepting a total cancellation, and `Engine::stop`'s emit can land in the read's leading post. No probe drove it.
  - `run_logout_phase1`: D-OUT-6.
  - `run_liveness_loop` is not edited. Its handler returns without a state write, which is right for a cancelled sleep (D-536-4) and is enough here because the state is already recorded. Under an `Engine` and under a direct driver alike, the cancellation that reaches a liveness frame's store is `Session::close`'s, which has recorded `Disconnected` before it emits (D-536-2).
  - `Session::send` and `on_inbound_active_`: as the table says, with D-OUT-9's and D-CAN-3's reset after each handler.
- **D-OUT-6 (R-17). The logout phase does not wait for a reply to a Logout it did not send.**
  - **Why at once, and for every outcome (R-17, both answers).** A reply cannot come to a Logout that was not sent, so the wait can only end at its timeout. The owner kept "close at once" after being shown that QuickFIX C++ and QuickFIX/J keep the connection until their logout timeout in that case (§4.5), and named the scope: "for every not-sent route".
  - **The condition.** `run_logout_phase1` goes on to record `LogoutSent` and to its wait only when `store_then_emit` returned success for the Logout. On every other outcome it leaves at once. The outcomes, read from the function and from `store_then_emit`:
    - the store call was cancelled (this section; an exception, caught at the call, as any exception out of that call is, route (w0) of D-CAN-6's case 2 included);
    - the store failed on a persistent store (059; the store's error returned);
    - the write failed, or the callback transport's send threw (`dispatch_aborted` returned);
    - the Logout could not be built (no call is made).
  - **A session with neither a live transport nor a callback sink keeps today's wait (the round's C-2).** For such a session `store_then_emit` reports every frame as sent: its transmit step writes through the live transport when there is one, calls the callback sink when one is configured, and otherwise runs no statement and returns success (read). The Logout's emit therefore succeeds, and the phase records `LogoutSent` and waits out its timeout, as today. It is read as outside R-17's routes: there is no transport that the Logout could have been handed to, and `store_then_emit` cannot tell this case from a frame that was sent without a new result value (Codex's counter-proposal; declined under `## Gate A`, and listed for the owner in §10's block).
    - **Who has such a session.** A `Session` driven directly with no `transport_send` configured. Under an `Engine` a session has its live transport installed before it can reach `Active` (reasoned by the round's triage from `drive_reconnect` and `attach_accepted_transport`).
    - **What depends on the wait.** Recipe: `git grep -l -E 'close_mode::graceful|[])A-Za-z_0-9](\.|->)close\(\)' -- tests` (the second alternative is there because `close`'s default mode is graceful), less the files that name `transport_send`; in each file left, a search for `on_inbound_frame` and for `fsm_state::Active`, `fsm_state::LogoutSent` and `fsm_state::LogonReceived`, which a cell needs to bring a session to a state the logout phase runs from. The files left with such a hit, each read: `tests/session/fsm_transition_matrix_test.cpp`, whose cell `FsmTransitionMatrixTest.LogoutSent_InboundNonLogoutEventsAreDrainedCellRemainsLogoutSent` drives a session with no sink to `Active`, spawns a graceful close and feeds a frame "during phase 1"; `tests/session/engine_reset_unit_stop_test.cpp` and `tests/session/engine_firstframe_test.cpp`, which drive an `Engine` on real transports; `tests/support/pump_until_ready.hpp`, whose hits are comments. The other files left have no such hit, so their sessions do not reach a state the phase runs from. The recipe is lexical: a file that names `transport_send` for one fixture and builds another session without it is not seen. The instrument for the suite is the full `ctest` at the exit of §10's phase.
  - **What the phase does then.** It records `Disconnected`, when the state is not already `Disconnected`; it returns the error (the store's; `dispatch_aborted` for a cancelled store or a failed write; the builder's own for a failed build); it does not record `LogoutSent`, and arms no wait.
  - **`logout_seen_` is set where it is set today:** after the emit step, whatever the emit returned, and so also on the paths that now leave early. The phase's existing early returns, for a throwing `toAdmin` and for a failed assign, do not set it, as today. So the teardown reset of `reset_on_logout` runs as it does today. R-17 rules on the wait and not on that reset. Noticed, not changed (§8): the flag's comment says it is true "whenever the Logout frame was actually placed on the wire", and it is set for a Logout that was not.
  - **What `close` does.** The phase's return ends `close`'s race with the grace timer at once, whichever arm the race then reports. `close` discards the phase's result, finds the state `Disconnected`, and goes on to its terminal part: the cancellation of the session's work, the transport's close, the drains. `close(graceful)` returns success, as it does after a logout timeout today. A `close(terminal)` that escalated meanwhile joins it and returns the same result at the same time. Neither waits `logout_disconnect_timeout_ms`.
  - **What the peer sees:** no Logout, and the connection closed. With a held earlier frame, that frame, if its store completes while the transport is still open.
  - **How it sits with `InboundFrameDispositionsOd29.EngineStopDuringAGracefulLogoutClosesAtOnce`.** That cell pins a terminal close that arrives while the phase is already in its wait: the grace timer is cancelled, the phase's wait ends, and the close completes within the cell's bound. It keeps its verdict: the Logout there was sent. Probe 4's scene is the case the cell does not reach, a Logout whose store was cancelled before the wait, where today the escalating close waits a full timeout; this decision gives that case the cell's ending.
  - **With D-536-3.** The wait that D-536-3 rewrites is reached only on the success outcome. The early return is ahead of the point where the wake is set, so D-536-3's rule that the wake is cleared on every exit of the wait has no new exit to cover.
    - **One cell of D-536-3 needs another fixture.** §5's control "D-536-3, the `try` around the race" runs the `catch` by cancelling the phase while its Logout emit is held in a hooked store. That trace reaches the race only when the emit then succeeds, and after this section it does not:
      - `tests/support/hooked_store.hpp` holds a `store` by a loop of posts that follows the inner store's call. A cancellation recorded on the thread during that loop is met by the loop's next pre-check, and leaves the wrapper's body as `operation_aborted` after the frame was stored. At the base `store_then_emit` turns that into a discarded `dispatch_aborted` and the phase goes on to the race. After D-OUT-2 it is a cancelled store, and the phase leaves before the race. Read from the wrapper.
      - On a live transport a cancellation that the store did not report is met at the transmit step (D-CAN-6, case 2, route (w0)), and the emit fails as well.
      - **The fixture after v0.9:** the callback transport, whose transmit is a synchronous call, and a wrapper whose hold cannot be ended by a cancellation: it clears the thread's throw-if-cancelled flag for its own awaits while it holds and restores it before it returns its inner result. The store then returns success with the cancellation recorded, the emit succeeds, and the race's pre-check throws. Reasoned; the cell is GREEN at the base on that fixture too.
    - **The hooked store's hold is the shape D-OUT-7 calls non-conforming**: it reports a cancellation after it has stored. Cells that cancel during such a hold are found by running (§10.4).
  - **The cancelled write is already immediate today.** When the grace timer or an escalation cancels the phase during its Logout's write, the cancellation stays recorded and the phase's wait throws at its pre-check (D-536-3, "The race sits inside a `try`"). This decision makes that case leave before the wait.
- **D-OUT-7 (R-15). The store contract says what `store_cancelled` means for `store()`.** `message_store.hpp` names no cancellation today; the per-method contract is only in `.specify/2e-msgstore.md`'s cancellation table, which this section returns the session to: its row for `store` says a cancelled call persisted nothing and that no transmission happened.
  - **The sentence the `store` comment gains:** `store_cancelled` means a cancellation ended the call before the frame was stored; the store then holds no frame for `seq` and its next number for that direction is unchanged. The same holds when the call ends by letting `operation_aborted` out. The session does not transmit the frame, takes the number back and ends. The comment also says, for every method of the interface, what `.specify/2e-msgstore.md`'s table says: a call that a cancellation ended before it took effect returns `store_cancelled` (D-CAN-4's condition).
  - **The order sentence the same comment gains (R-21).** The `store()` calls that one session makes for one direction return in the order the session made them; a call that a cancellation ended may return earlier. "D-OUT-7's sentences", elsewhere in this note, are this one and the one above. D-OUT-3's take-back and D-OUT-10's compare are exact under it (D-OUT-10, "Why the condition identifies the frame"). Both shipped stores keep it, read (the round's triage, C-1): `MemoryStore::store` makes one post, takes its lock and runs its sequence check, with no await under the lock; `FileStore::store` makes one post and waits for its lock, runs the same check, and has its one later await, the offload, on the path that stored. One corner is outside it and has no effect: after a `reset()` that failed past its rename the `FileStore` fails every call, a later one ahead of one still queued at the lock, and none of them is transmitted. The `FileStore` half is pinned by a control (§5). The `MemoryStore` half has no cell: nothing can hold a call inside it, so a cell there cannot fail.
  - **A store that does not keep the first sentence** (it reports a cancellation after it has stored). Its next number is one above the session's. The session has ended. A fresh session on a persistent store hydrates from the store, so it skips the number; the frame is in the store and was never transmitted, and a peer's ResendRequest for it draws a replay. A session reconnected in place on a store that is not persistent presents the number again; the store refuses it as out of order, and on such a store the frame is transmitted and the store keeps the older frame under that number. Reasoned; it is the consequence of the non-conformance, and the B&L row for store implementers says so (§6.1).
  - **A store that does not keep the order sentence** (a store that is not persistent, and that returns its calls out of the order they were made). The result of each trace: one number twice on the wire. Reasoned (the round's triage, C-1); not pinned.
    - *A refusal that returns late.* Frame k and frame k + 1 are in their store calls. Frame k is cancelled and the number is taken back to k. The store has refused frame k + 1 and holds that result back. An inbound arm that was already running sends its two replies meanwhile, numbered k and k + 1, which leaves the next number above both. The refusal then returns; on a store that is not persistent the frame goes on to the transmit step, its number is below the next one, and the compare passes it. The peer reads k + 1 twice.
    - *A later call that returns first.* Frame k + 1's refusal returns while frame k is still in its store call, and the frame is transmitted over the refusal, as every refused frame is on such a store. Nothing has been lowered at that moment. Frame k is then cancelled, and the take-back lowers the number to k. The next two frames carry k and k + 1. The peer reads k + 1 twice.
    - Such a store already puts frames on the wire out of order today: a later frame's refusal is transmitted over while the earlier frame is still in its call. No test in `store_then_emit` restores order over it (D-OUT-10, "Not taken").
- **D-OUT-8. The superseded decision is named where it is stated** (§6.4 lists every document and what the documents phase does to each).
- **D-OUT-9 (R-16's ending, on both timings). `Session::send` leaves its caller's thread as the inbound entry does.**
  - **Why.** After D-OUT-2 both timings of a cancelled store reach `Session::send`'s catch. On the returned timing the store's lock has left the thread clear; on the thrown timing the cancellation is still recorded. Without a further statement the two timings would differ in that, and a direct driver's next `co_await` would throw on one and not on the other.
  - **The statement.** `Session::send`'s handlers only note that they ran. After them, the first statement is `co_await asio::this_coro::reset_cancellation_state()`, as in D-CAN-3; then `Disconnected` is recorded, when the state is not already `Disconnected`, and `dispatch_aborted` is returned. The handlers record it unconditionally today; the test is new, because `store_then_emit` has already recorded the state on this route and every call of `record_state_transition_` adds an entry to the state ring. It runs for every exception the catch takes, so every cancellation that ends a send leaves the caller's thread in one state.
  - **What changes for today's thrown routes of a send** (a cancellation met by a pre-check at `send_impl`, at the sequence-number lock, or, after D-OUT-2, at the store call): the calling thread is clear when `Session::send` returns, where it is still cancelled today. The result and the session's state do not change. Under an `Engine` nothing observes it: the lambda that awaits `Session::send` awaits nothing after it (read from `Engine::send`).
  - **A send that fails without an exception is not touched**: a result that `send_impl` returns takes the path it takes today, and no reset runs for it. A cancelled write that the transmit step reports as a value is among those (D-CAN-6, case 2, on the send path: `dispatch_aborted`, `Disconnected`, the thread as the route left it); it is fixpp#584's.
  - **The dependency on fixpp#583** is D-CAN-3's: the two timings are equal because the reset installs what a cancelled lock wait leaves.
  - Not taken: leaving the thrown timing's thread cancelled. The two timings would then differ in the calling thread's state after `Session::send`.

### 10.3 Rejected

- **A returned code from `store_then_emit` on both timings, with each discarding caller changed to return it.** A list of sites, one per discarding caller of D-OUT-4's table, in leaves whose arms would each have to be read for what they do with a leaf's error. D-OUT-2's exception needs no caller to cooperate.
- **A returned code, with the thread's state reset inside `store_then_emit` so that both timings continue alike.** Both timings would then continue the arm after a cancelled store: a later reply of the same arm could go out.
- **059's reconcile read for the number.** D-OUT-3: on the thrown timing it cannot be awaited before the handler has ended and the state has been reset, and the reset is the entry point's, after `store_then_emit` has left.
- **Leaving the number consumed**, as both timings do today. R-16's premise.
- **v0.9's take-back, which left the counter alone when a later number had been assigned.** Withdrawn (R-19; D-OUT-3): on a store that is not persistent the number then stayed consumed and the later frame went out.
- **An issue and a batch of its own for the two-frames case.** Not chosen (R-19); the probe passed, so the ruling's fallback does not apply.
- **The number's reservation serialised through the store call, or a ledger of reserved numbers** (Codex's counter-proposals in review index 6). D-OUT-10, "Not taken".
- **An issue and a batch of its own for the initiator's Logon emission.** Not chosen (R-20).
- **`dispatch_aborted` from the handler in `emit_initiator_logon_`**, v0.9's code. D-OUT-5: a cancelled hydration read would then return one code or the other by timing.
- **A result of `store_then_emit` that tells "no transport" from "transmitted"** (Codex's counter-proposal for a session with no sink). D-OUT-6: the wait is kept for such a session, and the owner sees it at sign-off.
- **The exception without the state write in `store_then_emit`.** The liveness loop's handler would then end the loop with the session `Active` for a store that reports a cancellation nobody emitted.
- **Session stays up, as QuickFIX.** Not chosen (R-16).
- **R-17's rule for the cancelled store only.** Not chosen (R-17, the second answer: "Only the cancelled store").
- **QuickFIX's timing for a Logout that was not sent** (keep the connection until the logout timeout). Not chosen (R-17, the second answer).
- **The absorb kept for `Engine::stop`** (059's D7 rationale: a spurious disconnect during shutdown). `Engine::stop` sends no frame of its own and closes every session terminally; a reply cut by the stop is a frame the peer does not get on either timing after this section, and the session is being closed. The one shipped path on which a last frame rested on the absorb is the graceful Logout (measured, Probe 4), which R-17 rules.

### 10.4 Existing cells whose verdict changes

- **`StoreFailReconcileReadFailTest.StoreCancelled_NotFatal_StaysActiveAndTransmits`** (ctest `session_store_tests`). A persistent store double returns `store_cancelled` from `store()`. It asserts that the send succeeds, that the state is `Active`, and that the frame was transmitted. Predicted by D-OUT-1: all three change. It becomes the RED-first cell "D-OUT-1, a store that returns `store_cancelled`" (§5). The comment above that group in `tests/session/CMakeLists.txt` names the excluded leg and changes with it.
- **`CancelledOutboundStoreMeasurement.Returned_StoreCancelledAtItsLock`** (T0A.13). It pins today's returned timing. Predicted by D-OUT-1: not transmitted, `dispatch_aborted`, `Disconnected`, and no GapFill is asked for. `Thrown_CancelledAtTheStoreCallsFirstSuspension` keeps its assertions and gains the number's; `Control_NotCancelled_FrameStoredAndReplayed` keeps its verdict. The three become §5's pair and its control.
- **The cell of `tests/session/test_544_lock_cells.cpp` that cancels a `MemoryStore::store` in its leading post** calls `store_then_emit` through the test seam and asserts that its result is `dispatch_aborted`. Predicted by D-OUT-2: the call throws `operation_aborted`. What fixpp#544 wrote it to protect is that the cancellation is not erased before the lock; that is still asserted, as "the call does not succeed and nothing is transmitted".
- **Cells that keep their verdict, read:** `SendPathTest.Send_StoreThrowsOperationAborted_ReturnsDefinedError_NoTerminate` and `SendPathTest.Send_ThrowFromStore_SessionReachesDisconnected` (a store that throws, under `Session::send`: an error and `Disconnected`, as now); the send-slot cell of `test_544_send_slot_cells.cpp` that asserts `dispatch_aborted` and `Disconnected` from `Session::send`; the cells of 059's fail-closed route, whose parameter lists do not name `store_cancelled`.
- **Cells that cancel a coroutine while `tests/support/hooked_store.hpp` holds its `store`.** The hold lets the cancellation out of the wrapper's body after the frame was stored (D-OUT-6, "With D-536-3"), which D-OUT-2 reads as a cancelled store. §5's control for D-536-3's `try` is one, and gets another fixture. The others are found by running; each is re-read for whether it meant a store that reports a cancellation or a hold.
- **How the rest is found.** `git grep -n -E 'dispatch_aborted|store_cancelled' -- tests`, each hit read for whether it drives a cancelled store through `store_then_emit`. The hits outside the files above were read: they are a cancelled or failed write, the cancellable dispatch, the stores' own cancellation contract, and the C-ABI mapping. The recipe is lexical, so the instrument for the suite is the phase's full `ctest`, compared by name with the baseline. Tests that close gracefully over a transport that fails, or with `reset_on_logout` set, are the ones D-OUT-6 can change; they are found the same way.
- **For v0.10's changes.** The code of the Logon root (D-OUT-5): `git grep -n 'dispatch_aborted' -- tests`, the hits that name `open`, a Logon or a reconnect, read. `tests/session/initiator_transport_throw_test.cpp` pins `dispatch_aborted` from `open` when the callback transport's send throws; that is the transmit step's returned value, which D-OUT-5 does not change. No hit pins the code of a cancellation thrown at the Logon's store. The compare (D-OUT-10): the full debug `ctest` with Probe 8's candidate had no failure caused by it (measured). Both statements are lexical or in the probe's form; the instrument for the final form is the full `ctest` at the exit of §10's phase.

### 10.5 Every public awaitable of `Session`, and what a cancellation at a store call or a lock inside it ends as

Added in v0.10 (the round's RC-2). R-14 names the inbound entry and R-15 the store step, and v0.9 took its populations from those words. The class is wider: a public awaitable of `Session` whose body, or a coroutine it awaits, awaits a store call or a lock.

- **How it was derived.** `command grep -n 'asio::awaitable' include/fixpp/session/session.hpp`, each hit classed by the access section that declares it; the public ones are the rows. For each row, every `co_await` of the function's own body was listed, and then the store calls and the locks it reaches through the coroutines it awaits. Re-derive it the same way; a new public awaitable gets a row before it gets a caller.
- **"Fixed here"** means: after this batch a cancellation met at a store call or a lock inside the entry point has one ending on both timings, returns a value and throws nothing. The last column holds what is not that, and whose it is.

| Entry point | Reaches a store call or a lock through | A cancellation met there, both timings, after this batch | Fixed where | What is left, and whose it is |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `on_inbound_frame` | every arm (§9.2) | `Disconnected`, `store_cancelled`, nothing thrown, the calling thread clear; for a superseded Logon arm, nothing recorded and success | here: D-CAN-1 to D-CAN-5; for a reply's store, D-OUT-2 | A reply's transmit step: fixed elsewhere by ruling R-18 (fixpp#584; D-CAN-6, case 2). A cancellation recorded when the caller awaits the entry: the session has not started the frame (D-CAN-6, case 1) |
| `send` | `send_impl`: the sequence-number lock and the store call | `Disconnected`, `dispatch_aborted`, nothing thrown, the calling thread clear | here: its existing catch, with D-OUT-2 and D-OUT-9 | The transmit step, when it reports a cancelled write as a value: fixpp#584 (D-OUT-9, "A send that fails without an exception"). A cancellation recorded when the caller awaits `send` is met in the caller's frame, before `send` is entered: the session has not started the send |
| `open` | `emit_initiator_logon_`, for an initiator that is not engine-managed | `Disconnected`, `store_cancelled`, nothing thrown, the calling thread clear | here: D-OUT-5, as R-20 widens it | The Logon's transmit step: fixpp#584. `open`'s own body awaits `emit_initiator_logon_` and `close(terminal)`, and nothing else (read). Its await of `emit_initiator_logon_` follows no suspension of `open`'s body, so its pre-check can meet only a cancellation that the caller's own await of `open` met first. Its await of `close(terminal)`, after a throwing `onCreate`, has a pre-check that throws only when a cancellation is still recorded after a Logon emission that succeeded; only a transport write that accepted one and still completed leaves that (§9.2, "When a cancellation can be recorded"), which is the transmit step's leftover and fixpp#584's class. Both reasoned, not run |
| `drive_reconnect` | `emit_initiator_logon_` | as `open` | here: D-OUT-5, as R-20 widens it | **Fixed in fixpp#585 by ruling (R-22; batch B47, after B28 merges).** Its body awaits the connect attempt and then `emit_initiator_logon_`, with no `try` (read). The routes of a cancellation that reaches it ahead of the Logon emission, read from `drive_reconnect_attempt` and reasoned from asio's pre-check (the round's triage, C-3; not run). **(a)** The attempt reads the thread's cancellation state at its head, after its backoff wait and at each loop head, and returns `transport_connect_cancelled`. None of those reads clears the state, so on this returned route the cancellation is still recorded when `drive_reconnect` returns. **(b)** A cancellation that ends a connect or a handshake with an error: the loop goes round; with a backoff delay above zero the timer's await throws `operation_aborted`, and with a zero delay the loop-head read returns `transport_connect_cancelled`. **(c)** A cancellation recorded during a connect that still succeeded: the handshake's await throws; or, on a plaintext transport, the transport is installed, `install_reconnected_transport` records `LogonSent`, and the pre-check of `drive_reconnect`'s await of `emit_initiator_logon_` throws. On that last route the session has written state and holds a live transport when the exception leaves it. So one cancellation ends as a returned error or as a thrown exception, by timing. None of these awaits is a store call or a lock of the session, so they are outside this table's class and outside R-20's words. **Under an `Engine`** (read: `run_connect_loop` accepts a total cancellation, and `Engine::stop` emits one): on every route above the connect loop ends by exception today, the thrown ones directly and the returned one at the pre-check of its `close(terminal)`; the session was never published and is destroyed by the unwinding. After this batch, with `reset_on_disconnect` set, a stop that lands inside the Logon emission returns an error with the thread clear, the loop's `close(terminal)` runs, and a persistent store's numbers are reset to 1; a stop that lands a moment earlier, in the connect, still unwinds with no close and no reset. The next connection's Logon differs. Reasoned, not run. §10's block, item 12; §8 |
| `close` | the flush, the logout phase's Logout, the teardown reset, the drains | not reachable on `close`'s own thread: its first statement disables cancellation for its own awaits (read). The logout phase runs on a thread of `close`'s race, which the grace timer or an escalation cancels: a cancelled store of its Logout is D-OUT-6's, and the phase leaves at once | here: D-OUT-6 | Nothing leaves `close` by an exception: an exception that left the phase would be dropped by `close`'s race, whose other arm cannot throw (asio's `operator||` ignores an arm that ends with an exception unless both did). A cancellation recorded when the caller awaits `close` is met in the caller's frame, before `close` is entered (§9.2's row) |

- **Each reset after a handler rests on what a lock leaves on its caller's thread** (D-CAN-3, D-OUT-9, D-OUT-5): fixpp#583, batch B45, by the dependency §8 states.
- **What this table does not cover:** an await that is neither a store call nor a lock, in a function that is not on the inbound path. The connect attempt is the one found in the bodies of the rows; it is fixpp#585's (R-22). §9.2 covers every kind of await on the inbound path.

### Round 1 — root causes

- **RC-1.** D-537-5 was analysed against the inbound path only; the liveness loop is a second writer of the same outcome. Fix: §4.6, one table of every ending, with §4.4, §4.5, §5 and §6 written from it; D-537-6 (R-6).
- **RC-2.** D-538-4 extended fixpp#424's ruling to a different failure and did not shape the event for it. Fix: R-7. The answer aborts, and no event is widened.
- **RC-3.** Open questions were left in §7 instead of being decided. Fix: each is now a sentence in a decision plus a cell, or an owner item; §7 keeps only what is still open.
- **RC-4.** Populations were listed from memory of the recipe's output, not from the output. Fix: each recipe was re-run and every hit classified (§2.1, §2.3, §3.5, D-537-5's refresh sites, §4.2).

### Round 1 — disagreements

Codex's fix is not applied for these.

- **C-2's counter-proposal** (keep AwaitingResend until the recorded end is crossed after a SequenceReset). It changes `apply_inbound_sequence_reset`'s existing exit, which neither #537 nor a ruling covers. Its premise is also wrong: a partial GapFill advances NextNumIn, which is the progress R-2 measures.
- **C-3's counter-proposal** (close the transport at expiry and assert EOF). That is fixpp#534, batch B27. Every other Logout-then-`Disconnected` arm would then differ from this one. The note discloses instead, and the owner agreed.
- **C-8's second half** ("sessions sleep on their session executor and the clock keys the slot by `Session*`"). False for sessions under an Engine: their loops are not spawned on a `session_executor`. §3.3's sentence stands.
- **C-10's generation identity.** A Session runs the logout wait once; there is no older waiter to guard against.
- **C-11's "third-party implementation break".** A store that fails D-538-2 already shows #538 today, and the constitution makes no C++ API promise. A B&L row is enough.
- **C-12's "stop probing after the first I/O error, then gap-fill the rest".** R-7 subsumes the first half: the walk stops. It rejects the second: nothing is gap-filled that the store failed to read.
- **C-5's "commit the resend state only after successful dispatch", as a P2.** The premise is right; the reach is a few bytes of buffer margin. Kept at P3, with D-537-7.

### Round 2 — root causes

Numbered as in round 2's triage, with the prefix R2 to keep them apart from round 1's.

- **R2-RC-1. Each new rule was attached to one arm, and a frame above NextNumIn has several exits.** The deadline and the Heartbeat rule lived only in the out-of-sequence arm. Collapses C-1, O-1, C-5 and O-4. Fix: §4.7, a table of every exit of the Active inbound arm, read from `on_inbound_active_`; the deadline check as one helper with its own "above NextNumIn" and "a resend is outstanding" tests, called at every surviving exit (D-537-5); the Heartbeat rule at the frame that opens the gap (D-537-8, R-9); §4.6 scoped and derived from the table.
- **R2-RC-2. §0 restated rulings in this note's words, and the restatement was narrower or wider than what the owner said.** R-7 had become a set of two codes (C-2); "when a too-high frame arrives" had become "reaches the sequence gate" (C-1); R-6's gate was the consult's predicate (O-1). Fix: §0 quotes the owner, and this note's reading sits under each quotation, marked as a reading.
- **R2-RC-3. Conditionals were left inside §5 and §6.2.** "A cell only if", "depends on §7 item 1", "the assertion follows the owner's answer", "not traced". Collapses C-4, C-2's cell and row, and O-5. Fix: each was resolved by reading. D-537-7 has a mandatory cell built from the builders' arithmetic; slot 0 has an assertion (R-10); the untraced D-536-3 cell has its trace; the LogonReceived cell is waived with its reason; §6.2's store row has one classification. What cannot be settled without running is in §5's table of implementation steps, each with the condition that sends the design back.
- **R2-RC-4. Populations were listed by example** (round 1's RC-4 again). The exits ahead of the gate (C-1), and the paths on which a failed `on_inbound_frame` closes the transport (O-2). Fix: §4.7 was derived by reading the arm from top to bottom; D-537-9's members are its complement; the callers of `on_inbound_frame` were listed by `git grep` and each one's handling of a failed result was read (D-538-4).
